  "short_description": {
    "id": "Aderyn y Corff (\"burung mayat\" dalam bahasa Welsh) adalah burung dalam folklor Welsh yang menjadi pertanda kematian.",
    "en": "The Aderyn y Corff (Welsh for 'corpse bird') is a bird of Welsh folklore that foretells death.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "aderyn-y-corff-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Aderyn y Corff, dalam bahasa Welsh berarti \"burung mayat\" (juga Aderyn corff, Deryn corff, atau kuno Aderyn y Corph), adalah burung dalam folklor Welsh yang menandakan kematian. Ia konon berkicau di depan pintu orang yang akan segera meninggal dengan suara mirip kata dewch (\"datanglah\"). Burung ini tidak berbulu dan tidak bersayap; bila tidak sedang berseru di depan pintu, ia dikatakan hidup di alam lain.",
      "en": "The Aderyn y Corff, Welsh for 'corpse bird' (also Aderyn corff, Deryn corff, or archaically Aderyn y Corph), is a bird of Welsh folklore that heralds death. It is said to call outside the door of someone soon to die with a cry like dewch, 'come'. It has neither feathers nor wings, and when not crying at a door it is said to dwell on another plane.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aderyn-y-corff-c01",
        "aderyn-y-corff-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Burung ini disebut dua kali dalam Alkitab berbahasa Welsh, yang oleh sebagian penulis dianggap asal takhayul tersebut. Banyak kisah modern menduga burung mayat sebenarnya burung hantu yang tertarik oleh cahaya dari kamar orang sakit. Artikel es menyebut salah satu sumber penting tentangnya adalah British Goblins (1881) karya Wirt Sikes.",
      "en": "It is mentioned twice in the Welsh-language Bible, which some authors take to be the root of the superstition. Many later accounts suppose the corpse bird was really a screech owl drawn by light from a sick person's room. The es article names Wirt Sikes's British Goblins (1881) as a key source on it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aderyn-y-corff-c03",
        "aderyn-y-corff-c04",
        "aderyn-y-corff-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Basty atau Bastı adalah jin mimpi buruk dalam mitologi Turki, Altai dan Tatar, dengan beberapa jenis seperti Albastı dan Kulbastı.",
    "en": "The Basty or Bastı is a nightmare jinn of Turkish, Altai and Tatar mythology, with several kinds such as the Albastı and Kulbastı.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "basty-c01",
      "basty-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Basty atau Bastı, juga disebut Bastırık, adalah jin mimpi buruk dalam mitologi Turki, Altai dan Tatar. Mimpi buruk, tubuh lemas, terbangun ketakutan dan napas tersengal dikaitkan dengan roh jahat ini; dalam bahasa Turki Anatolia, \"bastırık\" berarti mimpi buruk. Namanya berasal dari akar kata bas-, \"menekan\". Ia dapat berganti rupa dan sangat suka menyamar sebagai kucing.",
      "en": "The Basty or Bastı, also called Bastırık, is a nightmare jinn of Turkish, Altai and Tatar mythology. Bad dreams, weakness, waking in terror and losing one's breath are blamed on this malign spirit, and in Anatolian Turkish 'bastırık' simply means a nightmare. The name comes from the root bas-, 'to press down'. It can change its form and especially likes to appear as a cat.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "basty-c01",
        "basty-c02",
        "basty-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Beberapa makhluk terkait membawa nama ini: Albastı, makhluk perempuan jahat yang tinggi, berkulit putih dan telanjang; Kulbastı, yang diyakini tidur di kubur pada siang hari dan bangkit malam hari, berbulu, sangat besar dan berbau busuk; serta Karabastı, jin mimpi buruk yang menindih dada orang pada malam hari hingga napasnya terhenti.",
      "en": "Several related beings bear the name: the Albastı, a tall, pale, naked and devilish female; the Kulbastı, believed to sleep in the grave by day and rise at night, hairy, huge and foul-smelling; and the Karabastı, the nightmare jinn that sits on people's chests at night until they cannot breathe.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "basty-c03",
        "basty-c04",
        "basty-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "basty-c01",
      "source_id": "basty-s1",
      "quote": "Bastı - Türk, Altay ve Tatar mitolojilerinde kabus cini. Bastırık (Bastırığ, Bastırı) da denilir. İnsanların kâbus görmesi, halsizleşmesi, korkuyla uyanması, sonrasında nefesinin kesilmesi bu kötücül ruh ile bağlantılı görülür.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Basty/Bastı (Bastırık), jin mimpi buruk mitologi Turki (Türk), Altai (Altay), Tatar; mimpi buruk, lemas, sesak napas; Anatolia: bastırık = mimpi buruk.",
        "en": "Basty/Bastı (Bastırık), nightmare jinn of Turkish, Altai, Tatar myth; nightmares, weakness, breathlessness; Anatolian: bastırık = nightmare."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basty-c02",
      "source_id": "basty-s1",
      "quote": "Değişik şekillere girebilir. Kedi kılığına bürünmeyi çok sever.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dapat berubah wujud, suka menyamar sebagai kucing.",
        "en": "Can change shape, loves cat disguise."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basty-c03",
      "source_id": "basty-s1",
      "quote": "Albastı: \"Aleybanı, Alyabani.\" Uzun boylu, beyaz tenli ve çıplak olarak tasvir edilen şeytani dişi varlık.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Albastı: perempuan jahat tinggi, berkulit putih, telanjang.",
        "en": "Albastı: a tall, white-skinned, naked devilish female."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basty-c04",
      "source_id": "basty-s1",
      "quote": "Kulbastı: \"Kuleybanı, Gulyabani.\" Gündüzleri mezarda uyuyup geceleri kalktığına inanılır. Tüylü, çok büyük ve pis kokulu olarak betimlenir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kulbastı: tidur di kubur siang, bangkit malam; berbulu, besar, bau.",
        "en": "Kulbastı: sleeps in the grave by day, rises at night; hairy, huge, smelly."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basty-c05",
      "source_id": "basty-s1",
      "quote": "(Bas) kökünden türemiştir. Basmak anlamını içerir. Hapishane anlamına da gelir. Ayrıca ağırlık demektir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari akar kata bas- (menekan); juga berarti penjara dan beban.",
        "en": "From the root bas- (to press); also prison and weight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "basty-c06",
      "source_id": "basty-s1",
      "quote": "Karabastı: Kâbus, kötü rüya. Kâbus cini olarak da bilinir. Geceleri insanların göğüslerine çökerek soluklarını keser.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karabastı: jin mimpi buruk yang menindih dada orang malam hari hingga napas terhenti.",
        "en": "Karabastı: nightmare jinn sitting on chests at night, stopping breath."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Billy Blind adalah roh rumah dari Inggris dan Dataran Rendah Skotlandia, mirip brownie, yang hanya muncul dalam balada sebagai pemberi nasihat.",
    "en": "Billy Blind is an English and Lowland Scottish household spirit, like a brownie, who appears only in ballads as an adviser.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "billy-blind-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Billy Blind (juga Billy Blin, Billy Blynde, Billie Blin atau Belly Blin) adalah roh rumah dari Inggris dan Dataran Rendah Skotlandia, mirip brownie. Ia hanya muncul dalam balada, sering sebagai penasihat para tokoh. Ia mungkin kenangan rakyat tentang dewa Woden atau Odin dalam \"aspeknya yang lebih jenaka\", dan diduga tokoh yang sama dengan Blind Harie di Skotlandia.",
      "en": "Billy Blind (also Billy Blin, Billy Blynde, Billie Blin or Belly Blin) is a English and Lowland Scottish household spirit, much like a brownie. He is found only in ballads, where he often counsels the characters. He may be a folk memory of the god Woden or Odin in his 'more playful aspect', and is thought to be the same as Blind Harie in Scotland.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "billy-blind-c01",
        "billy-blind-c02",
        "billy-blind-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Child Ballads, ia memberi tahu pahlawan \"Gil Brenton\" bahwa pengantinnya bukan perempuan di sampingnya; dalam \"Willie's Lady\" ia menyarankan Willie membuat boneka lilin bayi untuk mematahkan sihir ibunya yang menghalangi persalinan istrinya; dalam \"Young Bekie\" ia membantu Burd Isobel. Dalam fantasi modern ia muncul dalam Tamsin (1999) karya Peter S. Beagle dan serial Renegade Nell (2023), diperankan Nick Mohammed.",
      "en": "In the Child Ballads he tells the hero of 'Gil Brenton' that his bride is not the woman beside him; in 'Willie's Lady' he advises Willie to make a wax baby to break the spell by which his mother blocks his wife's labour; in 'Young Bekie' he aids Burd Isobel. In modern fantasy he appears in Peter S. Beagle's Tamsin (1999) and the series Renegade Nell (2023), played by Nick Mohammed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "billy-blind-c03",
        "billy-blind-c04",
        "billy-blind-c05",
        "billy-blind-c06"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Blunderbore adalah raksasa dalam folklor Cornwall dan Inggris, paling terkenal dari dongeng \"Jack the Giant Killer\".",
    "en": "Blunderbore is a giant of Cornish and English folklore, best known from the tale 'Jack the Giant Killer'.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "blunderbore-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Blunderbore (juga Blunderboar, Thunderbore, Blunderbus atau Blunderbuss) adalah raksasa folklor Cornwall dan Inggris, muncul dalam sejumlah dongeng, terutama \"Jack the Giant Killer\". Kisahnya biasanya dikaitkan dengan daerah Penwith. Folklor Cornwall mengingatnya tinggal di Ludgvan Lese, tempat ia meneror para pelancong menuju St Ives. Dalam \"Jack the Giant Killer\" ia raksasa kedua atau ketiga, bersama saudaranya Rebecks, yang dibunuh Jack; namanya juga sering dipakai untuk ogre dalam \"Jack and the Beanstalk\".",
      "en": "Known too as Blunderboar, Thunderbore, Blunderbus or Blunderbuss, Blunderbore is a giant from Cornish and English folklore who turns up in several tales, above all 'Jack the Giant Killer'. The stories usually place him around Penwith, and Cornish lore has him living at Ludgvan Lese, preying on people travelling north to St Ives. Jack kills him, alongside his brother Rebecks, as his second or third giant in 'Jack the Giant Killer', and the name is often given to the ogre of 'Jack and the Beanstalk'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "blunderbore-c01",
        "blunderbore-c02",
        "blunderbore-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam versi Joseph Jacobs, Blunderbore menculik tiga bangsawan beserta istrinya, berniat memakan para lelaki dan memperistri para perempuan. Ia menangkap Jack yang tertidur, tetapi Jack menjerat Blunderbore dan Rebecks dengan tali lalu menggorok leher mereka. Dalam dongeng Cornwall \"Tom the Tinkeard\", Blunderbore memagari jalan raya antara St Ives dan Marazion dan menculik sedikitnya dua puluh perempuan.",
      "en": "In Joseph Jacobs's version Blunderbore seizes three lords and their ladies, meaning to eat the men and wed the women. He captures the sleeping Jack, but Jack noose-snares Blunderbore and Rebecks with rope and cuts their throats. In the Cornish tale 'Tom the Tinkeard' Blunderbore hedges off the King's Highway between St Ives and Marazion and has abducted at least twenty women.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "blunderbore-c03",
        "blunderbore-c04",
        "blunderbore-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "id": "Bodach (\"lelaki tua\") adalah tokoh penipu atau momok dalam folklor dan mitologi Gaelik, pasangan cailleach dalam legenda Irlandia.",
    "en": "A bodach ('old man') is a trickster or bogeyman figure of Gaelic folklore and myth, paired with the cailleach in Irish legend.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bodach-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bodach (jamak bodaich, \"lelaki tua; orang desa, kasar\"; Irlandia Kuno botach) adalah tokoh penipu atau momok dalam folklor dan mitologi Gaelik. Dalam legenda Irlandia ia dipasangkan dengan cailleach, \"nenek tua\". Bodach adalah kata Irlandia untuk penyewa tanah, budak tani atau petani, dari bod (\"ekor, penis\") ditambah akhiran -ach; dalam bahasa Gaelik modern bodach hanya berarti \"lelaki tua\", sering dengan rasa sayang.",
      "en": "In Gaelic folklore and myth the bodach (plural bodaich; Old Irish botach), whose name means an old man or a coarse rustic, is a trickster or bogeyman figure, set alongside the cailleach, 'hag', in Irish legend. Bodach is the Irish for a tenant, serf or peasant, built from bod, 'tail, penis', and the suffix -ach; in modern Gaelic it just means 'old man', often fondly.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bodach-c01",
        "bodach-c02",
        "bodach-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Gaelik modern, bodach menjadi semacam momok hingga disamakan dengan iblis. Dalam kisah Eachtra Bhodaigh an Chóta Lachtna (abad ke-16 atau ke-17), ia disamakan dengan Manannán mac Lir. Dalam folklor Skotlandia ia turun lewat cerobong untuk menculik anak nakal, sebagai cerita peringatan. Makhluk terkait, Bodach Glas (\"Lelaki Tua Kelabu\"), dianggap pertanda kematian; dalam novel Waverley karya Walter Scott, Fergus Mac-Ivor melihatnya sebelum ajalnya.",
      "en": "In modern Gaelic lore the bodach becomes a kind of bugbear, even identified with the devil. The 16th- or 17th-century tale Eachtra Bhodaigh an Chóta Lachtna equates him with Manannán mac Lir. Scottish folklore has him come down the chimney to carry off naughty children, a warning tale. A related being, the Bodach Glas ('Old Grey Man'), is a death omen; in Walter Scott's Waverley Fergus Mac-Ivor sees one before his death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bodach-c03",
        "bodach-c04",
        "bodach-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "statement": {
        "id": "Bodach (bodaich; botach Irlandia Kuno, Old Irish), penipu atau momok folklor Gaelik (Gaelic); pasangan cailleach dalam legenda Irlandia (Irish).",
        "en": "Bodach (bodaich; Old Irish botach), trickster or bogeyman of Gaelic folklore; paired with the cailleach in Irish legend."

    {
      "id": "bodach-c02",
      "source_id": "bodach-s1",
      "quote": "Bodach (Old Irish also botach ) is the Irish word for a tenant, a serf or peasant. It is derived from bod (Old Irish bod ) \"tail, penis\".",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bran dan Sceólang (\"gagak\" dan \"penyintas\") adalah anjing-anjing Fionn mac Cumhaill dalam Siklus Fenian mitologi Irlandia.",
    "en": "In the Fenian Cycle of Irish myth, Bran and Sceólang, whose names mean 'raven' and 'survivor', are the dogs of Fionn mac Cumhaill.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bran-and-sceolang-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bran dan Sceólang, yang berarti \"gagak\" dan \"penyintas\", adalah anjing Fionn mac Cumhaill dalam Siklus Fenian mitologi Irlandia. Mereka digambarkan sebagian besar putih dengan pinggul ungu, ekor merah tua, kaki biru, dan setinggi bahu Fionn; sumber lain menyebut Bran bertelinga merah, berpunggung hijau dan berkaki kuning. Bran biasanya jantan dan Sceólang betina.",
      "en": "Bran and Sceólang, 'raven' and 'survivor', are Fionn mac Cumhaill's hounds in the Fenian Cycle of Irish mythology. They are described as mostly white, with purple haunches, a crimson tail and blue feet, standing as high as Fionn's shoulder; other sources give Bran red ears, a green back and yellow feet. Bran is usually male and Sceólang usually female.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bran-and-sceolang-c01",
        "bran-and-sceolang-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ibu mereka, Uirne, diubah menjadi anjing saat hamil, sehingga anak kembarnya lahir sebagai anjing; Uirne kembali menjadi manusia setelah melahirkan, tetapi Bran dan Sceólang tetap anjing. Karena Uirne saudari ibu Fionn, Muirne, kedua anjing itu sepupu tuannya. Menurut artikel ga, merekalah yang menemukan Oisín, putra Fionn, telanjang di hutan; Sceólang mati saat memburu rusa, dan Bran mati tenggelam setelah dipukul Fionn.",
      "en": "Their mother Uirne was turned into a dog while pregnant, so her twins were born as hounds; Uirne regained human form after the birth, but Bran and Sceólang stayed dogs. Since Uirne was sister to Fionn's mother Muirne, the hounds were their master's cousins. The ga article says they found Fionn's son Oisín naked in the forest; Sceólang died in a deer hunt, and Bran drowned himself after Fionn struck him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bran-and-sceolang-c03",
        "bran-and-sceolang-c04",
        "bran-and-sceolang-c05",
        "bran-and-sceolang-c06"
      ]
    }

    "id": "Buwch Frech (\"sapi berbintik\") adalah sapi dalam folklor Welsh yang memberi susu kepada siapa pun yang membutuhkan.",
    "en": "Y Fuwch Frech ('the speckled cow') is a cow of Welsh folklore that gave milk to anyone in need.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "buwch-frech-c01",
      "buwch-frech-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Y Fuwch Frech, dalam bahasa Welsh berarti \"sapi berbintik\", adalah sapi dalam folklor Welsh, yang oleh Elias Owen disebut \"Freckled Cow\". Dalam esainya untuk eisteddfod nasional 1887, Owen mencatat kisah dari Denbighshire tentang sapi yang memberi susu kepada siapa saja yang membutuhkan hingga seorang penyihir memerahnya sampai kering; sapi itu lalu pergi, terjun ke danau dekat Cerrig-y-drudion diikuti dua anaknya, sapi jantan bertanduk panjang.",
      "en": "Y Fuwch Frech, Welsh for 'the speckled cow', is a cow of Welsh folklore that Elias Owen called the 'Freckled Cow'. In his essay for the 1887 national eisteddfod Owen recorded a Denbighshire story of a cow that gave milk to anyone in want until a witch milked her dry; she then left, plunging into a lake near Cerrig-y-drudion with her two calves, long-horned oxen, following her.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "buwch-frech-c01",
        "buwch-frech-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gagasan sapi peri bersusu melimpah yang datang dari dan kembali ke danau terdapat di seluruh Wales. Owen mengaitkannya dengan sapi serupa di Shropshire, The White Cow of Mitchell's Fold, yang juga memberi seember susu sampai seorang penyihir memerahnya kering. Artikel cy menyebutnya juga Buwch Fraith Hiraethog, dari daerah Mynydd Hiraethog, putih bersih dengan bintik merah muda.",
      "en": "The notion of fairy cows rich in milk that come from and return to lakes is found all over Wales. Owen linked his cow with others such as one in Shropshire, the White Cow of Mitchell's Fold, which likewise gave a pail of milk to all comers until a witch milked her dry. The cy article also calls her Buwch Fraith Hiraethog, from the Mynydd Hiraethog area, pure white with pink speckles.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "buwch-frech-c03",
        "buwch-frech-c04",
        "buwch-frech-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "Y Fuwch Frech (Welsh: literally the speckled/brindled cow) is a cow in Welsh folklore. She was described by Elias Owen as the \"Freckled Cow\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Y Fuwch Frech (Buwch Frech, sapi berbintik), sapi folklor Welsh (Wales); Elias Owen: Freckled Cow.",

      }

    {
      "id": "buwch-frech-c02",
      "source_id": "buwch-frech-s1",
      "quote": "he recorded a story from Denbighshire of a cow which was said to give milk to \"any one ... in want of milk\" until a witch milked the cow dry. The cow then left, plunging into a lake near Cerrig-y-drudion, and leading her two children, long-horned oxen",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Esai eisteddfod 1887; Denbighshire: susu untuk yang membutuhkan; penyihir memerah kering; terjun ke danau dekat Cerrig-y-drudion dengan dua anak sapi jantan (Ychen Bannog).",
        "en": "1887 eisteddfod essay; Denbighshire: milk for the needy; a witch milked her dry; plunged into a lake near Cerrig-y-drudion with two oxen (Ychen Bannog)."

    {

    {

    {
      "id": "buwch-frech-c05",
      "source_id": "buwch-frech-s2",
      "quote": "Yn ei draethawd ar lên gwerin, ar gyfer Eisteddfod Genedlaethol 1887, disgrifia Elias Owen stori am fuwch hudol o ardal Mynydd Hiraethog. Roedd yn wyn pur gyda brychni pinc ac roedd ganddi gyflwenwad diddiwedd o lefrith.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cauld Lad of Hylton adalah hantu pemuda kandang Robert Skelton yang dibunuh, konon menghantui reruntuhan Kastel Hylton di Sunderland, Inggris Utara.",
    "en": "The Cauld Lad of Hylton is said to be the spirit of Robert Skelton, a stable lad who was murdered, and to haunt the ruined Hylton Castle in Sunderland, Northern England.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cauld-lad-of-hylton-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cauld Lad of Hylton adalah hantu Robert Skelton, pemuda penjaga kandang yang dibunuh, yang konon menghantui reruntuhan Kastel Hylton di Sunderland, Inggris Utara. Peristiwanya dikatakan terjadi pada abad ke-16 atau ke-17. Satu versi menyebut ia ketahuan mendekati putri Baron Hylton; versi lain menyebut sang baron murka karena Skelton kesiangan, lalu membunuhnya dan membuang jasadnya ke kolam atau sumur.",
      "en": "The Cauld Lad of Hylton is the ghost of Robert Skelton, a murdered stable boy, said to haunt the ruins of Hylton Castle in Sunderland in the north of England. The events supposedly took place in the 16th or 17th century. In one version he was caught courting Baron Hylton's daughter; in another the baron, furious that Skelton had overslept, killed him and threw the body into a pond or well.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cauld-lad-of-hylton-c01",
        "cauld-lad-of-hylton-c02",
        "cauld-lad-of-hylton-c06"

    },
    {
      "id": "Setelah itu dapur kastel dirapikan di malam hari bila berantakan, atau diacak-acak bila rapi. Seorang juru masak yang berjaga melihat hantu anak lelaki telanjang yang menangis \"I'm cauld\" (kedinginan); ketika diberi jubah hangat, hantu itu pergi dan gangguan berhenti. Perilakunya mirip poltergeist, dan versi lain menyebutnya elf, barghest atau brownie yang terkena kutukan dan hanya bebas bila diberi hadiah.",
      "en": "Afterwards the castle kitchen would be tidied at night if left messy, or upset if left neat. A cook who stayed up saw the ghost of a naked boy crying 'I'm cauld' ('I'm cold'); once given a warm cloak the ghost left and the disturbances stopped. Its behaviour suggests a poltergeist, and other versions make the Cauld Lad an elf, barghest or brownie under a spell that only a gift can lift.",

        "cauld-lad-of-hylton-c04",
        "cauld-lad-of-hylton-c05"
      ]

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cofgod (jamak Cofgodas, \"dewa ruang\") adalah istilah bahasa Inggris Kuno untuk dewa rumah tangga dalam paganisme Anglo-Saxon.",
    "en": "In Anglo-Saxon paganism, cofgod (plural cofgodas, 'cove-gods') was the Old English name for a god of the household.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cofgod-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cofgod (jamak Cofgodas, \"dewa ruang\") adalah istilah bahasa Inggris Kuno untuk dewa rumah tangga dalam paganisme Anglo-Saxon. Ahli klasik Ken Dowden berpendapat cofgodas setara dengan Penates di Roma Kuno, dan membandingkannya dengan Kobold dalam folklor kontinental kemudian; menurutnya keduanya berasal dari kofewalt, roh yang menguasai sebuah ruangan.",
      "en": "Cofgod (plural Cofgodas, 'cove-gods') was the Old English name for a god of the home in Anglo-Saxon paganism. Ken Dowden, a classicist, held that the cofgodas matched the Penates of ancient Rome, and compared them with the Kobold of later continental folklore, both, he argued, stemming from the kofewalt, a spirit ruling over a room.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cofgod-c01",
        "cofgod-c02"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Bila makhluk semacam ini memang dikenal orang Inggris awal, maka makhluk legenda kemudian seperti hob Inggris dan brownie Anglo-Kelt bisa jadi sisa modern dari cofgod. Namun satu-satunya kemunculan kata cofgodas dalam bahasa Inggris Kuno adalah sebagai glos (definisi penjelas) untuk kata Latin penates.",
      "en": "If the early English did know such beings, later legendary figures like the English hob and the Anglo-Celtic brownie could be what survives of the cofgod today. Still, the word cofgodas occurs in Old English only once, as a gloss explaining the Latin word penates.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cofgod-c03",

        "cofgod-c05"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "cofgod-c01",
      "source_id": "cofgod-s1",
      "quote": "was an Old English term for a household god in Anglo-Saxon paganism.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cofgod (Cofgodas), istilah bahasa Inggris Kuno (Old English) untuk dewa rumah tangga paganisme Anglo-Saxon; Inggris.",
        "en": "Cofgod (Cofgodas), Old English term for a household god of Anglo-Saxon paganism; England."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cofgod-c02",
      "source_id": "cofgod-s1",
      "quote": "The classicist Ken Dowden opined that the cofgodas were the equivalent of the Penates found in Ancient Rome. Dowden also compared them to the Kobold of later continental folklore, arguing that they had both originated from the kofewalt, a spirit that had power over a room.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ken Dowden: setara Penates Roma Kuno (Ancient Rome); dibandingkan dengan Kobold; dari kofewalt, roh penguasa ruangan.",
        "en": "Ken Dowden: equivalent of the Penates of Ancient Rome; compared to the Kobold; from the kofewalt, spirit of a room."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cofgod-c03",
      "source_id": "cofgod-s1",
      "quote": "If it is true that such beings were known to the early English, later legendary beings such as the English hob and Anglo-Celtic brownie would be the modern survival of the cofgod.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hob Inggris (English) dan brownie Anglo-Kelt (Anglo-Celtic) mungkin sisa modern cofgod.",
        "en": "The English hob and Anglo-Celtic brownie may be modern survivals of the cofgod."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cofgod-c04",
      "source_id": "cofgod-s1",
      "quote": "However, the only instance of the word cofgodas in Old English is as a gloss (an explanatory definition) to the Latin word penates.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu-satunya kemunculan: glos untuk kata Latin penates.",
        "en": "Only instance: a gloss on Latin penates."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cofgod-c05",
      "source_id": "cofgod-s2",
      "quote": "코프갓(Cofgod)은 앵글로색슨 이교에서 가택신을 가리키던 고대 영어 용어이다.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel ko: istilah Inggris Kuno untuk dewa rumah dalam paganisme Anglo-Saxon.",
        "en": "ko article: Old English term for a household god in Anglo-Saxon paganism."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Naga Mordiford adalah makhluk legenda yang konon tinggal di luar desa Mordiford, Herefordshire, di pertemuan Sungai Lugg dan Sungai Wye.",
    "en": "The Dragon of Mordiford is a legendary creature said to have lived just outside Mordiford in Herefordshire, where the Lugg meets the Wye.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dragon-of-mordiford-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga Mordiford adalah makhluk legenda yang konon tinggal di luar desa Mordiford di Herefordshire, di pertemuan Sungai Lugg dan Sungai Wye; ada banyak versi tentang wujudnya. Dalam satu versi, naga itu seekor wyvern yang ditemukan gadis kecil bernama Maud di hutan dan diam-diam ia pelihara dengan susu. Naga itu tumbuh besar, berwarna hijau zamrud dan bersayap kuat, lalu mulai memangsa ternak dan bahkan para petani, tetapi tak pernah menyakiti Maud, satu-satunya sahabatnya.",
      "en": "The Dragon of Mordiford is a legendary creature said to have lived just beyond Mordiford in Herefordshire, where the River Lugg joins the River Wye, and accounts of its form differ. In one, it is a wyvern found in the woods by a little girl named Maud, who secretly raised it on milk. It grew huge, emerald-green and strong-winged, and began killing livestock and even farmers, but never harmed Maud, its only friend.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dragon-of-mordiford-c01",
        "dragon-of-mordiford-c02",
        "dragon-of-mordiford-c03",
        "dragon-of-mordiford-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang lelaki dari keluarga Garstone, berbaju zirah lengkap, menghadapinya, menangkis semburan apinya dan menusukkan tombak menembus lehernya; Maud datang meratapi hewan peliharaannya. Versi lain menyebut naga berbentuk ular; seorang narapidana yang dijanjikan pengampunan bersembunyi dalam tong sari apel di tepi Sungai Lugg dan memanahnya tepat di jantung, tetapi racun naga itu mengalir ke dalam tong dan membunuhnya.",
      "en": "A man of the local Garstone family, in full armour, faced it, turned aside its blast of fire and drove a lance clean through its throat, and Maud came out to mourn her pet. Another version makes the dragon serpent-like: a convict promised a pardon hid in a cider barrel by the River Lugg and shot it through the heart, but the dragon's poison ran into the barrel and killed him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dragon-of-mordiford-c04",
        "dragon-of-mordiford-c05",
        "dragon-of-mordiford-c06"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "The Dragon of Mordiford is a legendary creature that was said to reside just outside the Herefordshire village of Mordiford, at the confluence of the River Lugg and the River Wye.",

    {

      "source_id": "dragon-of-mordiford-s1",
      "quote": "One version of the legend explains that the dragon – a wyvern in this account – was found by a little girl from Mordiford named Maud, who had desperately wanted a pet.",

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "dragon-of-mordiford-c03",
      "source_id": "dragon-of-mordiford-s1",
      "quote": "Soon, it began to plague the local farms, killing their livestock, especially cows and sheep, and farmers were intolerant.",

    {
      "id": "dragon-of-mordiford-c04",
      "source_id": "dragon-of-mordiford-s1",

    },
    {
      "id": "dragon-of-mordiford-c05",
      "source_id": "dragon-of-mordiford-s1",
      "quote": "He then concealed himself in the barrel at the River Lugg, where the serpent drank its water. Once the dragon arrived at the river, the convict shot an arrow through a hole in the barrel, piercing the creature's heart.",

    {

        "drummer-of-cortachy-c01"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Penabuh Drum Cortachy adalah roh yang konon menghantui Kastel Cortachy di Skotlandia dan menandakan kematian keluarga Ogilvy, Earl of Airlie.",
    "en": "The Drummer of Cortachy is a spirit said to haunt Cortachy Castle in Scotland, foretelling deaths in the Ogilvy family of the Earl of Airlie.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "drummer-of-cortachy-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Penabuh Drum Cortachy adalah nama roh yang dipercaya menghantui Kastel Cortachy. Kisahnya punya banyak tafsir, tetapi ia umumnya dianggap menandakan kematian anggota keluarga Ogilvy, Earl of Airlie, atau pemilik kastel itu. Tingginya konon sembilan kaki, kadang diiringi bunyi pipa hantu, dan ia memainkan tattoo (tabuhan) saat muncul. Legenda ini setidaknya berakar dari abad ke-19 dan kematian Earl of Airlie ke-14.",
      "en": "The Drummer of Cortachy is the name of a spirit believed to haunt Cortachy Castle. Readings of the story vary, but he is generally thought to herald the death of a member of the Ogilvy family, the Earl of Airlie, or the castle's owners. He is said to stand nine feet tall, sometimes with ghostly pipes, and to beat a tattoo when he appears. The tale can be traced back no later than the 1800s, to when the 14th Earl of Airlie died.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "drummer-of-cortachy-c01",
        "drummer-of-cortachy-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hantu ini dianggap roh penabuh drum keluarga Carlisle yang dilempar dari jendela menara tinggi kastel karena kecemburuan sang Lord; versi lain menyebutnya utusan kepala suku yang dibenci, atau penabuh yang sengaja tak memperingatkan serangan. Dalam semua versi ia dijejalkan ke dalam drumnya sebelum mati. Penampakan paling terkenal terjadi pada 1844, ketika Lady Airlie mendengar bunyi drum misterius; ia meninggal pada Juni 1845. Roh ini kini dianggap tidur sejak Earl of Airlie gugur dalam Perang Boer tahun 1900 tanpa ada penampakan.",
      "en": "He is thought to be the ghost of a drummer of the Carlisle family thrown from a window of the castle's high tower after rousing the then Lord's jealousy; other versions make him a hated chieftain's messenger or a drummer who deliberately failed to warn of an attack. In every version he was stuffed into his drum before he died. The best-known sighting came in 1844, when Lady Airlie heard the mysterious drumming; she died in June 1845. The spirit is now thought dormant, since the Earl of Airlie fell in the Boer War in 1900 with no reported sighting.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "drummer-of-cortachy-c03",
        "drummer-of-cortachy-c04",
        "drummer-of-cortachy-c05",
        "drummer-of-cortachy-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "drummer-of-cortachy-c01",
      "source_id": "drummer-of-cortachy-s1",
      "quote": "The Drummer of Cortachy is the name given to a spirit who is thought to haunt Cortachy Castle. There are many interpretations of the story, but he is variously thought to portend the death of a member of the Ogilvy family, Earl of Airlie or the owners of Cortachy Castle. He is said to be nine foot tall",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penabuh Drum Cortachy (Drummer of Cortachy), roh di Kastel (Castle) Cortachy, Skotlandia; pertanda kematian keluarga Ogilvy, Earl of Airlie; sembilan kaki; pipa hantu.",
        "en": "Drummer of Cortachy, a spirit of Cortachy Castle, Scotland; omen of death for the Ogilvy family, Earl of Airlie; nine feet; ghostly pipes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drummer-of-cortachy-c02",
      "source_id": "drummer-of-cortachy-s1",
      "quote": "The legend can trace its roots back to at least the 19th century and the death of the 14th Earl of Airlie. He is said to play a tattoo when he appears.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akar abad ke-19, kematian Earl of Airlie ke-14 (14th); memainkan tattoo.",
        "en": "Roots in the 19th century, death of the 14th Earl of Airlie; plays a tattoo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drummer-of-cortachy-c03",
      "source_id": "drummer-of-cortachy-s1",
      "quote": "The ghost is thought to be the spirit of a drummer for the Carlisle family; who was thrown out of a window in the high tower of the castle after incurring the jealousy of the then Lord.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh penabuh drum keluarga Carlisle dilempar dari menara karena cemburu Lord; dijejalkan ke dalam drum.",
        "en": "Spirit of a Carlisle family drummer thrown from the tower by a jealous Lord; stuffed into his drum."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drummer-of-cortachy-c04",
      "source_id": "drummer-of-cortachy-s1",
      "quote": "The most famous sighting of the drummer is said to have occurred in 1844, when Lady Airlie (or, in some versions of the tale, her guests) reportedly heard the mysterious drumming noise.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penampakan 1844, Lady Airlie mendengar drum; meninggal Juni 1845.",
        "en": "1844 sighting, Lady Airlie heard drumming; died June 1845."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drummer-of-cortachy-c05",
      "source_id": "drummer-of-cortachy-s1",
      "quote": "The spirit is now thought to be dormant since in 1900 the Earl of Airlie died in the Boer War without a reported sighting.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh kini tidur; Earl of Airlie gugur di Perang Boer (Boer War) 1900.",
        "en": "The spirit dormant; the Earl of Airlie died in the Boer War, 1900."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drummer-of-cortachy-c06",
      "source_id": "drummer-of-cortachy-s2",
      "quote": "১৯০০ চনত বোৱাৰ যুদ্ধত কোনো ধৰণৰ ৰহস্যময় শব্দ নোহোৱাকৈয়ে আৰ্লিৰ আৰ্লৰ মৃত্যু হোৱাৰ বাবে বৰ্তমান এই আত্মাভাগ সুপ্ত হৈ থকা বুলি ধৰা হয়।",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel as: roh dianggap tidur sejak kematian Earl of Airlie dalam Perang Boer 1900.",
        "en": "as article: spirit dormant since the Earl of Airlie's death in the Boer War, 1900."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ellén Trechend adalah monster berkepala tiga dalam mitologi Irlandia yang keluar dari gua Cruachan dan dibunuh penyair-pahlawan Amergin.",
    "en": "The Ellén Trechend is a three-headed monster of Irish myth that came out of the cave of Cruachan and was killed by the poet-hero Amergin.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ellen-trechend-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ellén Trechend adalah monster berkepala tiga dalam mitologi Irlandia. Menurut teks Cath Maige Mucrama (Pertempuran Mag Mucrima), ia keluar dari gua Cruachan (Rathcroghan, County Roscommon) dan menghancurkan Irlandia hingga dibunuh penyair dan pahlawan Ulaid, Amergin. Namanya sulit ditafsirkan: trechend berarti \"berkepala tiga\", tetapi ellén kata yang kabur. Seorang penerjemah mengartikannya \"kawanan makhluk berkepala tiga\", Whitley Stokes \"burung raksasa berkepala tiga\", dan T. F. O'Rahilly menyamakannya dengan Aillén, monster penyembur api yang dilawan Fionn mac Cumhaill.",
      "en": "The Ellén Trechend is a three-headed monster of Irish mythology. The text Cath Maige Mucrama (The Battle of Mag Mucrima) says it came out of the cave of Cruachan (Rathcroghan, County Roscommon) and ravaged Ireland until the Ulaid poet and hero Amergin killed it. The name is hard to read: trechend means 'three-headed', but ellén is obscure. One translator gives 'swarm of three-headed creatures', Whitley Stokes 'monstrous triple-headed bird', and T. F. O'Rahilly links it with Aillén, the fire-breathing foe of Fionn mac Cumhaill.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ellen-trechend-c01",
        "ellen-trechend-c02",
        "ellen-trechend-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "P. W. Joyce menceritakan bahwa Sidhe Cruachan terbuka pada Samhain dan dari sana menyerbu kerumunan goblin mengerikan bersama kawanan burung merah tembaga yang dipimpin burung nasar raksasa berkepala tiga. Burung nasar itu sangat mungkin Ellén Trechend, karena burung merah tembaga juga disebut dalam Cath Maige Mucrama.",
      "en": "P. W. Joyce tells how the Sidhe of Cruachan opened at Samhain and a horde of hideous goblins poured out, together with copper-red birds led by a monstrous three-headed vulture. That vulture may well be the Ellén Trechend, since the copper-red birds also appear in Cath Maige Mucrama.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ellen-trechend-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "The Ellén Trechend is a three-headed monster referred to in Irish mythology.       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ellén Trechend, monster berkepala tiga mitologi Irlandia (Irish, Ireland); Cath Maige Mucrama (Pertempuran Mag Mucrima); gua Cruachan (Rathcroghan, County Roscommon); dibunuh Amergin, penyair Ulaid.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fions adalah makhluk mirip lutin dari bangsa kecil dalam folklor maritim Haute-Bretagne, pelayan para peri houle.",
    "en": "Fions are lutin-like little people of the maritime folklore of Upper Brittany, serving the houles fairies.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fions-c01",
      "fions-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fions adalah makhluk mirip lutin dari bangsa kecil, terutama disebut dalam folklor maritim Haute-Bretagne, dan mungkin berasal dari Inggris. Sebagian besar kisah tentang mereka berasal dari kumpulan Paul Sébillot pada akhir abad ke-19. Mereka tinggal di batu karang dan gua pantai utara Bretagne, hidup secara militer bersama para peri houle sebagai pelayan mereka; tersusun dalam batalion, konon mereka berperang melawan sebuah kapal emas.",
      "en": "Fions are lutin-like beings of the little people, mostly found in the maritime folklore of Upper Brittany and perhaps English in origin. Most stories about them come from Paul Sébillot's late 19th-century collections. Living among the rocks and caves of Brittany's northern coast, they lead a soldierly life alongside the houles fairies as their servants, and, formed into battalions, are said to make war on a golden ship.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fions-c01",
        "fions-c02",
        "fions-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut kisah-kisah, mereka memiliki dan menggembalakan ternak, dan kadang memberi benda ajaib atau makanan kepada manusia. Paul Sébillot menyatakan tidak ada Fions betina, setidaknya di houle, sedangkan Pierre Dubois menganggap mereka hermafrodit. Françoise Morvan melihat mereka berkerabat dengan peri Anglo-Saxon seperti Jetins.",
      "en": "Tales say they own and graze livestock and sometimes give people enchanted objects or food. Paul Sébillot states there were no female Fions, at least in the houles, whereas Pierre Dubois thinks them hermaphrodite. Françoise Morvan sees them as related to Anglo-Saxon fairies such as the Jetins.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fions-c03",
        "fions-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "fions-c01",
      "source_id": "fions-s1",
      "quote": "Fions are lutin-like creatures of the little people, mostly mentioned in the maritime folklore of Upper Brittany. They might be of English origin. Most of the stories about them come from Paul Sébillot's collections in the late 19th century.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fions, mirip lutin, bangsa kecil folklor maritim Haute-Bretagne (Upper Brittany, Prancis); mungkin asal Inggris (English); Paul Sébillot akhir abad ke-19.",
        "en": "Fions, lutin-like little people of Upper Brittany's maritime folklore; maybe English in origin; Paul Sébillot, late 19th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fions-c02",
      "source_id": "fions-s1",
      "quote": "Characterized by their habitat in the rocks and caves of Brittany's northern shores, the Fions lead a military life in community with the houles fairies, as their servants. Organized into battalions, they are said to wage war on a golden ship.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tinggal di batu dan gua pantai utara Bretagne (Brittany); pelayan peri houle; batalion; perang melawan kapal emas.",
        "en": "Live in rocks and caves of Brittany's north shore; servants of the houles fairies; battalions; war on a golden ship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fions-c03",
      "source_id": "fions-s1",
      "quote": "According to tales, they own and graze livestock, and sometimes give enchanted objects or food to humans.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menggembalakan ternak, memberi benda ajaib atau makanan.",
        "en": "Graze livestock, give enchanted objects or food."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fions-c04",
      "source_id": "fions-s1",
      "quote": "Opinions differ on this point, for while Paul Sébillot says \"there were no female Fions, at least in the houles\", Pierre Dubois believes they are hermaphroditic creatures.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paul Sébillot: tak ada Fions betina; Pierre Dubois: hermafrodit; Françoise Morvan: kerabat peri Anglo-Saxon, Jetins.",
        "en": "Paul Sébillot: no female Fions; Pierre Dubois: hermaphrodite; Françoise Morvan: kin of Anglo-Saxon fairies, Jetins."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fions-c05",
      "source_id": "fions-s2",
      "quote": "Les Fions sont des créatures du petit peuple surtout mentionnées dans le folklore maritime de la Haute-Bretagne, de type lutin et peut-être d'origine anglaise.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Firedrake dalam folklor Inggris adalah penampakan naga berupa bola api atau jejak api di langit, berasal dari bintang jatuh dan will-o'-the-wisp.",
    "en": "Firedrakes in English folklore are dragon-sightings seen as fireballs or fiery streaks in the sky, arising from shooting stars and will-o'-the-wisps.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "firedrakes-in-english-folklore-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Firedrake (juga fire-drake; Inggris Kuno: fyrdraca) dalam folklor Inggris adalah konsep yang kurang dikenal, tetapi masih muncul dalam cerita lama, puisi, bahkan karya Shakespeare. Firedrake adalah penampakan naga yang berasal dari bintang jatuh dan will-o'-the-wisp, tampak sebagai bola api berbentuk naga atau jejak api yang berpercik di langit; istilah ini juga bersinonim dengan \"naga api\" dan \"meteor berapi\". Firedrake sering dianggap sebagai Iblis sendiri dalam wujud naga.",
      "en": "Firedrakes (also fire-drake; Old English fyrdraca) are a little-known idea of English folklore that still crops up in old tales, verse and even Shakespeare. They are dragon-sightings born of shooting stars and will-o'-the-wisps, appearing as dragon-shaped fireballs or sparking trails of fire across the sky, and the word doubles for 'fiery dragon' and 'fiery meteor'. The firedrake was often taken to be the Devil himself in dragon form.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "firedrakes-in-english-folklore-c01",
        "firedrakes-in-english-folklore-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Istilah ini memakai bentuk asli drake (Inggris Kuno draca), bukan pinjaman Prancis dragon, dan muncul sejak Beowulf abad ke-8 hingga ke-9. Dalam Henry VIII, Shakespeare secara jenaka menyebut seorang tukang patri berhidung merah sebagai \"firedrake\". Artikel ja menyebut naga api dalam Beowulf (fyr draca) secara luas mewakili naga api Jermanik.",
      "en": "The term uses the native form drake (Old English draca) rather than the French loan dragon, and appears as early as Beowulf, of the 8th to 9th century. In Henry VIII Shakespeare jokingly calls a red-nosed brazier a 'firedrake'. The ja article treats the Beowulf fire dragon (fyr draca) as broadly standing for Germanic fire dragons.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "firedrakes-in-english-folklore-c03",
        "firedrakes-in-english-folklore-c04",
        "firedrakes-in-english-folklore-c05"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gaillimh inion Breasail adalah perempuan mitos yang namanya menjadi nama sungai dan kota Galway, Irlandia; kini dianggap dewi sungai setempat.",
    "en": "Gaillimh inion Breasail is the mythical woman who gave her name to the river and city of Galway, Ireland, now seen as a local river goddess.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c01",
      "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gaillimh inion Breasail adalah nama perempuan mitos yang menjadi asal nama sungai dan kota Galway di Irlandia. James Hardiman, mengutip Lughaidh Ó Cléirigh, menulis bahwa kota Galway dinamai menurut sungai tempat Gaillimh, putri Breasail, tenggelam. Hardiman menambahkan bahwa peta kota pertengahan abad ke-17 menunjukkan sebuah batu di sungai (kini Corrib) tempat seorang perempuan bernama Galva tenggelam.",
      "en": "Gaillimh inion Breasail is the mythical woman after whom the river and city of Galway in Ireland are named. James Hardiman, quoting Lughaidh Ó Cléirigh, wrote that Galway took its name from the river in which Gaillimh, daughter of Breasail, drowned. Hardiman adds that a mid-17th-century map of the town shows a rock in the river, now the Corrib, where a woman named Galva was drowned.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c01",
        "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dikatakan putri Breasail, seorang kepala suku Fir Bolg. Kini umumnya diyakini bahwa ia dewi suku atau dewi sungai setempat, seperti dewi Gaelik lain misalnya Boann. Nama itu diperkirakan berarti kira-kira \"sungai berbatu\" dan mungkin berasal setidaknya dari akhir masa prasejarah Irlandia.",
      "en": "She was said to be the daughter of Breasail, a chief of the Fir Bolg. She is now widely thought to have been a tribal or local river goddess, like other Gaelic deities such as Boann.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c01",
      "source_id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-s1",
      "quote": "Gaillimh inion Breasail is the name of the mythical woman from whom the river and city of Galway, Ireland, derives its name. According to James Hardiman, quoting Lughaidh Ó Cléirigh, \"that the city of Galway took its name from the river, in which was drowned Gaillimh, the daughter of Breasail.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gaillimh inion Breasail, perempuan mitos asal nama sungai dan kota Galway, Irlandia (Ireland); James Hardiman mengutip Lughaidh Ó Cléirigh; tenggelam di sungai.",
        "en": "Gaillimh inion Breasail, the mythical woman behind the name of the river and city of Galway, Ireland; James Hardiman quoting Lughaidh Ó Cléirigh; drowned in the river."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c02",
      "source_id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-s1",
      "quote": "Hardiman goes on to state that in the mid-17th map of the town, \"a rock is shown in the river\" (now called the Corrib), \"where\" it is stated, that \"a woman, named Galva, was drowned, near a great rock, in the river,\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peta pertengahan abad ke-17: batu di sungai (Corrib) tempat perempuan bernama Galva tenggelam.",
        "en": "Mid-17th-century map: a rock in the river (Corrib) where a woman named Galva drowned."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c03",
      "source_id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-s1",
      "quote": "She was said to be the daughter of a chief of the Fir Bolg, Breasail. It is now generally held that she was a tribal or local goddess of the river, much like other Gaelic deities such as Boann.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Putri Breasail, kepala suku Fir Bolg; dewi sungai setempat seperti Boann (Gaelik, Gaelic).",
        "en": "Daughter of Breasail, a Fir Bolg chief; local river goddess like Boann (Gaelic)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c04",
      "source_id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-s1",
      "quote": "The etymology of the name is thought to translate, roughly, as stony river, and probably dates back to at least the late Irish pre-historic era.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama berarti sungai berbatu; prasejarah akhir Irlandia (Irish).",
        "en": "Name means stony river; late Irish prehistory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-c05",
      "source_id": "gaillimh-inion-breasail-s2",
      "quote": "Bean mhiotasach ab ea Gaillimh iníon Breasail as a ainmníodh cathair agus abhainn na Gaillimhe. Dúradh gurbh iníon cheannaire na bhFear Bolg, Breasail, í.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel ga: perempuan mitos asal nama kota dan sungai Galway; putri Breasail, kepala Fir Bolg.",
        "en": "ga article: mythical woman who named Galway city and river; daughter of Breasail, Fir Bolg chief."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Galloway Puma adalah \"kucing hantu\" dalam folklor Skotlandia yang konon hidup di Dumfries and Galloway, Skotlandia barat daya.",
    "en": "The Galloway Puma is a 'phantom cat' of Scottish folklore said to live in Dumfries and Galloway, south-west Scotland.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "galloway-puma-c01"
    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Skotlandia, Galloway Puma adalah \"kucing hantu\" yang konon tinggal di Dumfries and Galloway, Skotlandia barat daya. Desas-desus tentang kucing besar di Galloway bermula pada akhir 1990-an, ketika wisatawan Kanada mengaku melihat hewan mirip cougar dekat Kirroughtree, tenggara Newton Stewart, sepulang berjalan di Hutan Galloway. Warga Newton Stewart juga mengaku melihatnya pada 1999.",
      "en": "Scottish folklore places a 'phantom cat', the Galloway Puma, in the Dumfries and Galloway region of south-west Scotland. Talk of a big cat in Galloway started in the late 1990s, when Canadian tourists said they had seen a cougar-like animal near Kirroughtree, south-east of Newton Stewart, on the way back from a walk in Galloway Forest. Locals in Newton Stewart reported it too in 1999.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "galloway-puma-c01",
        "galloway-puma-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setelah itu banyak orang, terutama petani dan pekerja Forestry Commission, mengaku melihat kucing hitam besar di wilayah itu, terutama di daerah Machars. Pada Juni 2001, seorang perempuan yang mengajak anjingnya berjalan dekat lapangan golf Newton Stewart dikejutkan \"kucing hitam besar, lebih besar dari anjing alsatian\" yang melompat ke arahnya lalu lari ketika anjingnya menggonggong. Artikel zh mencatat penampakan lain pada Desember 2012 oleh Alan Dalton dan istrinya.",
      "en": "Since then many people, especially farmers and Forestry Commission staff, have claimed sightings of a big black cat across the region, mostly in the Machars. In June 2001 a woman walking her dog near Newton Stewart golf course was startled by a 'large black cat, bigger than an alsatian' that sprang out at her and fled when her dog barked. The zh article records another sighting in December 2012 by Alan Dalton and his wife.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "galloway-puma-c03",
        "galloway-puma-c04",
        "galloway-puma-c05"
      ]
    }

      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Raksasa (Welsh: cewri) sangat menonjol dalam folklor dan mitologi Welsh, seperti Bendigeidfran, Idris Gawr dan Ysbaddaden Bencawr.",
    "en": "Giants (Welsh cewri) are prominent in Welsh folklore and myth, among them Bendigeidfran, Idris Gawr and Ysbaddaden Bencawr.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "giants-welsh-folklore-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Raksasa (Welsh: cewri) sangat menonjol dalam folklor dan mitologi Welsh. Yang paling terkenal antara lain Bendigeidfran fab Llyr, raja mitologis Britania dalam Cabang Kedua Mabinogi; Idris Gawr dari Cader Idris; dan Ysbaddaden Bencawr, lawan utama dalam kisah Arthur awal How Culhwch won Olwen. Arthur dan Gwalchmai fab Gwyar menonjol sebagai pembunuh raksasa dalam tradisi Welsh, dan Geoffrey dari Monmouth menggambarkan raksasa sebagai penghuni asli Britania yang dikalahkan pemukim manusia.",
      "en": "Giants (Welsh cewri) loom large in Welsh folklore and mythology. Famous examples include Bendigeidfran fab Llyr, who rules Britain as a mythic king in the Mabinogi's Second Branch; Idris Gawr, linked with Cader Idris; and Ysbaddaden Bencawr, chief villain of the early Arthurian tale How Culhwch won Olwen. Arthur and Gwalchmai fab Gwyar both stand out as giant-killers in Welsh tradition, and Geoffrey of Monmouth calls giants the first inhabitants of Britain, overrun by human settlers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "giants-welsh-folklore-c01",
        "giants-welsh-folklore-c02",
        "giants-welsh-folklore-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Branwen ferch Llyr, Britania diperintah raksasa Bran the Blessed yang tak pernah muat di rumah mana pun, dan Pair Dadeni (kuali kelahiran kembali) dibawa ke Wales dari Irlandia oleh raksasa Llassar Llaes Gyfnewid. Dalam Culhwch and Olwen, Ysbaddaden, ayah Olwen, dibunuh keponakannya Goreu fab Custennin. Kisah terkenal lain menyebut Rhitta Gawr yang memakai jubah dari janggut raja-raja dan menuntut janggut Arthur.",
      "en": "In Branwen ferch Llyr the king of Britain is the giant Bran the Blessed, too large ever to fit inside a house, and the Pair Dadeni (Cauldron of Rebirth) is brought from Ireland to Wales by the giant Llassar Llaes Gyfnewid. In Culhwch and Olwen, Ysbaddaden, Olwen's father, is killed by his nephew Goreu fab Custennin. Another well-known tale tells of Rhitta Gawr, who wore a cloak of kings' beards and demanded Arthur's.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "giants-welsh-folklore-c03",

      "id": "giants-welsh-folklore-c01",
      "source_id": "giants-welsh-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Among the most notable are Bendigeidfran fab Llyr, a mythological king of Britain during the Second Branch of the Mabinogi, Idris Gawr of Cader Idris, and Ysbaddaden Bencawr, the chief antagonist of the early Arthurian tale How Culhwch won Olwen. Both Arthur and Gwalchmai fab Gwyar feature prominently as giant-slayers in Welsh tradition.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raksasa (cewri) Welsh (Wales): Bendigeidfran fab Llyr, raja Britania (Britain), Cabang Kedua Mabinogi (Second Branch); Idris Gawr, Cader Idris; Ysbaddaden Bencawr, How Culhwch won Olwen; Arthur, Gwalchmai fab Gwyar.",
        "en": "Welsh giants (cewri): Bendigeidfran fab Llyr, king of Britain, Second Branch of the Mabinogi; Idris Gawr, Cader Idris; Ysbaddaden Bencawr, How Culhwch won Olwen; Arthur, Gwalchmai fab Gwyar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "giants-welsh-folklore-c02",
      "source_id": "giants-welsh-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Giants are also described by Geoffrey of Monmouth as the original inhabitants of Britain, who were overwhelmed by human settlers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Geoffrey dari (of) Monmouth: penghuni asli Britania (Britain).",
        "en": "Geoffrey of Monmouth: the original inhabitants of Britain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "giants-welsh-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "giants-welsh-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "In the Second Branch of the Mabinogi, Branwen ferch Llyr, Britain is ruled by the giant Bran the Blessed, who has never been able to fit inside any dwelling.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Branwen ferch Llyr: Bran the Blessed; Pair Dadeni (Kuali, Cauldron of Rebirth), Llassar Llaes Gyfnewid dari Irlandia (Ireland).",
        "en": "Branwen ferch Llyr: Bran the Blessed; Pair Dadeni (Cauldron of Rebirth), Llassar Llaes Gyfnewid from Ireland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "giants-welsh-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "giants-welsh-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Ysbaddaden, chief of giants, is the father of Olwen, a beautiful maiden sought by Culhwch fab Cilydd, a cousin of King Arthur's. He is slain at the tale's close by his nephew Goreu fab Custennin",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Culhwch and Olwen: Ysbaddaden ayah Olwen, dibunuh Goreu fab Custennin.",
        "en": "Culhwch and Olwen: Ysbaddaden, Olwen's father, slain by Goreu fab Custennin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "giants-welsh-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "giants-welsh-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The twenty-six kings of Britain assembled their armies to destroy Rhitta but were vanquished by the giant, who cut off the kings' beards and fashioned a great cape out of them to protect him from the cold.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rhitta Gawr: jubah dari janggut raja-raja; menuntut janggut Arthur.",
        "en": "Rhitta Gawr: a cape of kings' beards; demanded Arthur's beard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "giants-welsh-folklore-c06",
      "source_id": "giants-welsh-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Mae cewri yn ymddangos yn rheolaidd yn llên gwerin a mytholeg Cymraeg.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel cy: raksasa (cewri) sering muncul dalam folklor dan mitologi Welsh.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gwragedd Annwn adalah peri perempuan cantik dalam folklor Welsh yang tinggal di bawah danau dan sungai, bagian dari Tylwyth Teg.",
    "en": "Gwragedd Annwn are beautiful female fairies of Welsh folklore who live beneath lakes and rivers, counted among the Tylwyth Teg.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gwragedd-annwn-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gwragedd Annwn (tunggal gwraig Annwn), juga disebut Dames of Elfin Land atau Wives of the Lower World, adalah peri perempuan cantik dalam folklor Welsh yang tinggal di bawah danau dan sungai. Mereka termasuk Tylwyth Teg, bangsa peri Welsh, dan kisah mereka terjalin dengan asal-usul sapi hitam Welsh. Kadang mereka naik ke dunia atas dan dapat dilihat orang biasa.",
      "en": "Gwragedd Annwn (singular gwraig Annwn), also called Dames of Elfin Land or Wives of the Lower World, are lovely female fairies of Welsh folklore living under lakes and rivers. They belong to the Tylwyth Teg, the Welsh fairy folk, and their story is bound up with the origin of the Welsh black cattle. At times they rise to the upper world and can be seen by ordinary people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gwragedd-annwn-c01",
        "gwragedd-annwn-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Konon mereka diciptakan St. Patrick saat ia pergi dari Irlandia ke Wales menemui St. David: orang-orang Welsh yang mencacinya diubah menjadi ikan, sedangkan para perempuan menjadi peri danau. Tidak seperti selkie atau merrow, mereka tidak bercirikan makhluk laut, dan hanya tinggal di danau dan sungai seperti Llyn Barfog di Gwynedd dan Danau Crumlyn dekat Briton Ferry; mereka berpakaian hijau.",
      "en": "They were supposedly made by St. Patrick on a journey from Ireland to Wales to see St. David: Welsh folk who abused him were turned into fish, and the women among them into lake fairies. Unlike selkies or merrows they have no sea-creature traits, living only in lakes and rivers such as Llyn Barfog in Gwynedd and Crumlyn Lake near Briton Ferry, and they dress in green.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gwragedd-annwn-c03",
        "gwragedd-annwn-c04",
        "gwragedd-annwn-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "The mythological narrative of Gwragedd Annwn is intertwined with the origin of the Welsh black cattle. Occasionally, the fairies were said to ascend into the upper world, and be visible to ordinary people.",

      "source_id": "gwragedd-annwn-s1",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hob adalah roh rumah tangga kecil dalam folklor Midlands Inggris, Inggris Utara dan perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia.",
    "en": "A hob is a small household spirit in the folklore of the English Midlands, Northern England and the Anglo-Scottish border.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hob-folklore-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hob adalah sejenis roh rumah tangga kecil dalam folklor tradisional Midlands Inggris, Inggris Utara dan perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia. Mereka bisa tinggal di dalam rumah atau di luar, bekerja di pekarangan pertanian sehingga bermanfaat, tetapi bila tersinggung bisa menjadi pengganggu. Cara biasa mengusir hob adalah memberinya satu setel pakaian baru, yang membuatnya pergi selamanya, walau hob terburuk mungkin tak bisa diusir.",
      "en": "A hob is a kind of small household spirit in the traditional folklore of the English Midlands, Northern England and the Anglo-Scottish border. Hobs might live indoors or out and worked about farmyards, making them useful, though if offended they turned into pests. The usual way to be rid of one was to give it a new suit of clothes, after which it left for good, though the worst hobs could not be shifted at all.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hob-folklore-c01",
        "hob-folklore-c02",
        "hob-folklore-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hob adalah bentuk kecil nama Robin, yang sendiri bentuk kecil Robert, dan kadang dipakai umum untuk goblin, bogle atau brownie. Di utara Inggris kata ini juga terkait dengan Inggris Kuno þyrs dan Norse Kuno þurs (\"raksasa\"), melahirkan varian hob-thrush dan hob-thrust. Di Yorkshire, hob digambarkan lelaki kecil berbulu dan keriput, baik tetapi nakal; hob terkenal di Runswick Bay tinggal di sebuah hobhole dan dipercaya menyembuhkan batuk rejan anak-anak.",
      "en": "Hob is a pet form of Robin, itself a pet form of Robert, and is sometimes a general word for a goblin, bogle or brownie. In the north of England it is also tied to Old English þyrs and Old Norse þurs, 'giant', giving forms such as hob-thrush and hob-thrust. Yorkshire hobs were small, hairy, wizened men, kindly but mischievous; a famous one at Runswick Bay lived in a hobhole and was believed to cure children of whooping cough.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hob-folklore-c03",
        "hob-folklore-c04",
        "hob-folklore-c05",
        "hob-folklore-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "hob-folklore-c01",
      "source_id": "hob-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "A hob is a type of small mythological household spirit found in the English Midlands, Northern England, and on the Anglo-Scottish border, according to traditional folklore of those regions. They could live inside the house or outdoors. They are said to work in farmyards and thus could be helpful; however if offended they could become nuisances.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hob, roh rumah kecil Midlands Inggris (English), Inggris Utara (Northern England), perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia (Anglo-Scottish); bekerja di pertanian; diusir dengan pakaian baru.",
        "en": "Hob, small household spirit of the English Midlands, Northern England, Anglo-Scottish border; works on farms; driven off by new clothes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hob-folklore-c02",
      "source_id": "hob-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "As well as the brownie, another cognate exists in the Scandinavian nisse or tomte; all are thought to be derived from the household gods of olden times, known in England as the cofgodas",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berkerabat dengan brownie, nisse, tomte; dari cofgodas (dewa rumah kuno Inggris).",
        "en": "Cognate with the brownie, nisse, tomte; from the cofgodas, old English house-gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hob-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "hob-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "‘Hob’ is a diminutive form of the name Robin, which is itself a diminutive form of the name Robert. Hob is sometimes a generic term given to a goblin, bogle or brownie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hob bentuk kecil Robin dan Robert; istilah umum goblin, bogle, brownie; þyrs (Inggris Kuno), þurs (Norse Kuno); hob-thrush, hob-thrust.",
        "en": "Hob, diminutive of Robin and Robert; generic for goblin, bogle, brownie; Old English þyrs, Old Norse þurs; hob-thrush, hob-thrust."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hob-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "hob-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "One famous hob lived near Runswick Bay in a hobhole; this hob was believed to be able to cure young children of kink-cough (whooping cough).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hob Runswick Bay di hobhole menyembuhkan batuk rejan.",
        "en": "The Runswick Bay hob in a hobhole cured whooping cough."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hob-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "hob-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Hobs have been described as small, hairy, wizened men. Hobs were viewed as kind but mischievous spirits, helpful to local people in need.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yorkshire: lelaki kecil berbulu, keriput; baik tetapi nakal; aktif malam.",
        "en": "Yorkshire: small, hairy, wizened men; kind but mischievous; about at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hob-folklore-c06",
      "source_id": "hob-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "In hob is in mytysk wêzen út 'e folkloare fan Ingelân.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel fy: hob, makhluk mitos folklor Inggris (Ingelân).",
        "en": "fy article: the hob, a mythical being of English folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hob-folklore-c07",
      "source_id": "hob-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The usual way to dispose of hobs was to give them a set of new clothing, the receiving of which would make the creatures leave forever.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hob diusir dengan diberi pakaian baru; pergi selamanya.",
        "en": "Hobs were disposed of by giving new clothes; they left forever."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lantern man adalah cahaya hantu dalam folklor The Fens di East Anglia, Inggris, yang dipercaya roh jahat penarik korban ke rawa alang-alang.",
    "en": "A lantern man is a ghost light of the folklore of the Fens of East Anglia, England, believed to be an evil spirit luring victims into the reed beds.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lantern-man-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lantern man adalah cahaya hantu di udara dalam folklor The Fens di East Anglia, dengan penampakan di sekitar Wicken Fen dan daerah lain. Menurut kisah yang pertama dikumpulkan folkloris L. F. Newman, cahaya itu dipercaya roh jahat yang berusaha menarik korban menuju kematian di rawa alang-alang. Roh itu tertarik oleh bunyi siulan, dan cara menghindarinya adalah berbaring telungkup dengan mulut di lumpur. Fenomena ini tampaknya variasi kisah will-o'-the-wisp dan kini dianggap penampakan gas rawa yang terbakar.",
      "en": "A lantern man is an atmospheric ghost light in the folklore of the Fens of East Anglia, reported around Wicken Fen and elsewhere. In stories first gathered by the folklorist L. F. Newman, the lights are wicked spirits trying to lure people to their deaths in the reed beds. Whistling attracts them, and the way to escape is to lie face down with your mouth in the mud. The phenomenon looks like a form of will-o'-the-wisp lore and is now put down to burning marsh gas.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lantern-man-c01",
        "lantern-man-c02",
        "lantern-man-c03",
        "lantern-man-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang nelayan setempat bercerita kepada parapsikolog Peter Underwood bahwa ia pernah menjatuhkan diri ke tanah untuk menghindari lantern man yang tertarik oleh siulannya. Seorang lelaki lain yang bersiul memanggil anjingnya di rawa berlindung di rumah temannya, yang menggantungkan sebuah tanduk pada tiang panjang untuk mengalihkan roh itu; paginya tanduk itu ditemukan hangus terbakar.",
      "en": "A local fisherman told the parapsychologist Peter Underwood that he once threw himself flat to escape a lantern man drawn by his whistling. Another man, whistling for his dog on the fen, sheltered at a friend's house; the friend hung a horn on a long pole to distract the spirit, and next morning the horn was found burnt up.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lantern-man-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lí Ban adalah perempuan yang menjadi putri duyung dalam legenda terbentuknya Lough Neagh, Irlandia, kemudian dibaptis sebagai Santo Muirgen.",
    "en": "Lí Ban is a woman turned mermaid in the legend of the forming of Lough Neagh, Ireland, later baptised as Saint Muirgen.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "li-ban-mermaid-c01",
      "li-ban-mermaid-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lí Ban atau Liban (dari bahasa Irlandia Kuno lí \"kecantikan\" dan ban \"perempuan\", berarti \"teladan para perempuan\") adalah perempuan yang menjadi putri duyung dalam legenda terbentuknya Lough Neagh. Keluarganya tenggelam saat danau besar itu menyembur, tetapi ia selamat setahun dalam ruang di bawah air, lalu berubah menjadi setengah manusia setengah salem. Menurut kisah Aided Echach maic Maireda dalam Lebor na hUidre abad ke-12, danau itu dinamai menurut ayahnya, Eochaid mac Mairidh.",
      "en": "Lí Ban or Liban (Old Irish lí, 'beauty', and ban, 'of women', so 'paragon of women') is a woman turned mermaid in the legend of how Lough Neagh came to be. Her family drowned when the great lake burst up, but she lived a year in an underwater chamber and then became half woman, half salmon. In the tale Aided Echach maic Maireda in the 12th-century Lebor na hUidre, the lake takes its name from her father Eochaid mac Mairidh.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "li-ban-mermaid-c01",
        "li-ban-mermaid-c02",
        "li-ban-mermaid-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam wujud putri duyung ia terlihat oleh kapal yang membawa utusan St. Comgall ke Roma, berjanji bertemu di Inbhear nOllarbha (Larne Lough) setahun kemudian, dan tertangkap jaring. Ia dibaptis Comgall dengan nama Muirgein (\"lahir dari laut\") atau Muirgeilt, dan tercatat sebagai Santo Muirgen dengan hari raya 27 Januari. Annals of the Four Masters mencatat penangkapannya pada 558 M. Artikel fi menyebutnya memilih langsung ke surga setelah dibaptis.",
      "en": "In mermaid form she was seen from the ship carrying St. Comgall's messenger to Rome, promised to meet at Inbhear nOllarbha (Larne Lough) a year later, and was caught in a fishnet. Comgall baptised her Muirgein ('sea-born') or Muirgeilt, and she is listed as Saint Muirgen with a feast on 27 January. The Annals of the Four Masters date her capture to 558 AD. The fi article says that after baptism she chose to go straight to heaven.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "li-ban-mermaid-c03",
        "li-ban-mermaid-c04",
        "li-ban-mermaid-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "Her family was drowned, but she survived in an underwater chamber in the lake for a year, after which she was transformed into a being who was half-human, half-salmon.",

      "source_id": "li-ban-mermaid-s1",
      "quote": "There she was baptised by Comgall, and given the Christened name Muirgein (\"sea-born\") or Muirgeilt (\"sea-wander\") . She appears as Saint Muirgen in genealogies of Irish saints with her feast day assigned to 27 January.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Dibaptis Comgall (St. Comgall): Muirgein/Muirgeilt; Santo (Saint) Muirgen, 27 Januari (January); utusan ke Roma (Rome); Inbhear nOllarbha (Larne Lough).",

      "source_id": "li-ban-mermaid-s1",
      "quote": "According to this old tale, Liban turned into a mermaid when a spring burst under her house to form Lough Neagh (Old Irish: Loch nEchach ), named after Liban's father Eochaid mac Mairidh who was drowned by the gushing water.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Maggy Moulach (Meg Mullach) adalah brownie Dataran Tinggi dalam folklor Skotlandia, ibu dari dobie Brownie-Clod, juga dijuluki Hairy Meg.",
    "en": "Maggy Moulach (Meg Mullach) is a Highland brownie of Scottish folklore, mother of the dobie Brownie-Clod, also called Hairy Meg.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "maggy-moulach-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Maggy Moulach (juga Meg Mullach, Maggie Moloch, Maug Moulach dan Hairy Meg) adalah tokoh folklor Skotlandia yang dikatakan brownie Dataran Tinggi. Ia punya putra bernama Brownie-Clod, seorang dobie, yaitu brownie yang agak lamban tetapi bermaksud baik. Ia digambarkan setinggi dua kaki dengan rambut lebat atau tangan berbulu, sehingga dijuluki Hairy Meg; dalam versi kemudian ia menjulurkan tangan lewat cerobong untuk mencuri anak, dan dapat berubah menjadi belalang.",
      "en": "Maggy Moulach (also Meg Mullach, Maggie Moloch, Maug Moulach and Hairy Meg) is a figure of Scottish folklore said to be a Highland brownie. Her son Brownie-Clod was a dobie, a rather slow but well-meaning kind of brownie. She stood two feet high with a great mass of hair or hairy hands, hence Hairy Meg; later versions have her reaching down chimneys to steal children, and she could turn into a grasshopper.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "maggy-moulach-c01",
        "maggy-moulach-c02",
        "maggy-moulach-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda awal, Meg dan putranya tinggal di kastel Tullochgorm milik keluarga Grant; ia mengerjakan tugas brownie, membantu kepala klan menang catur, dan bertindak sebagai banshee yang mengabarkan kematian keluarga itu. Dalam kisah lain, Brownie-Clod yang menjaga sebuah penggilingan disiram air mendidih oleh seorang gadis yang mengaku bernama \"Mi fein\" (diriku sendiri); ia mati, dan Maggy kemudian membalas dendam dengan melempar bangku hingga gadis itu tewas.",
      "en": "In early legends Meg and her son lived in Tullochgorm castle, owned by the Grant family; she did brownie work, helped the clan chief beat rivals at chess, and served as a banshee announcing deaths in the family. In another tale Brownie-Clod, guarding a mill, was scalded by a girl who gave her name as 'Mi fein', 'me myself'; he died, and Maggy later took revenge by hurling a stool that killed the girl.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "maggy-moulach-c03",
        "maggy-moulach-c04",
        "maggy-moulach-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "is a character from Scottish folklore said to be a Highland brownie. According to the folklore, she had a son named Brownie-Clod, who was said to be a dobie. A dobie is a somewhat dull-witted, though well-intentioned, variety of brownie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maggy Moulach (Meg Mullach, Maggie Moloch, Maug Moulach, Hairy Meg), brownie Dataran Tinggi (Highland) folklor Skotlandia (Scottish); putra Brownie-Clod, dobie.",
