    },
    {
      "id": "maggy-moulach-c02",
      "source_id": "maggy-moulach-s1",
      "quote": "She is described as a 2 ft 0 in (0.61 m) tall creature with an impressive head of hair or (and), as said in other lore, with hairy hands; that is why she was sometimes called Hairy Meg.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setinggi dua kaki, rambut lebat atau tangan berbulu, Hairy Meg; berubah menjadi belalang.",
        "en": "Two feet tall, thick hair or hairy hands, Hairy Meg; turns into a grasshopper."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maggy-moulach-c03",
      "source_id": "maggy-moulach-s1",
      "quote": "In the earlier legends, it is said that Meg and her son were living in Tullochgorm castle, which belonged to the Grant family. She performed the usual labors of brownie, but also helped the clan chief beat his opponents at chess.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maggy-moulach-c04",
      "source_id": "maggy-moulach-s1",
      "quote": "Keeping his distance, he asked for her name and the quick girl replied, \"Oh, I'm Mise mi fein\" which means \"Oh, I am me myself\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gadis mengaku bernama Mi fein (diriku sendiri) dan menyiram Brownie-Clod dengan air mendidih.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mooinjer veggey adalah istilah Manx untuk \"orang kecil\", sebutan peri dalam folklor Pulau Man.",
    "en": "Mooinjer veggey is Manx for 'little people', the term for fairies in Isle of Man folklore.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "mooinjer-veggey-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mooinjer veggey adalah bahasa Manx untuk \"orang kecil\", istilah untuk peri dalam tradisi Gaelik; padanannya dalam bahasa Irlandia dan Gaelik Skotlandia adalah Muintir Bheaga dan Muinntir Bheaga. Dalam folklor Manx mereka makhluk kecil setinggi 2–3 kaki, selain itu sangat mirip manusia; mereka memakai topi merah dan jaket hijau dan paling sering terlihat menunggang kuda diikuti kawanan anjing kecil beraneka warna pelangi. Mereka cenderung nakal dan pendendam.",
      "en": "Mooinjer veggey is Manx for 'little people', the Gaelic term for fairies, matched by Irish Muintir Bheaga and Scottish Gaelic Muinntir Bheaga. In Manx folklore they are small, 2–3 feet tall, but otherwise much like mortals; they wear red caps and green jackets and are usually seen on horseback with packs of little hounds in every colour of the rainbow. They tend to be mischievous and spiteful.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mooinjer-veggey-c01",
        "mooinjer-veggey-c02",
        "mooinjer-veggey-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka tinggal di lereng bukit hijau, terutama gundukan kubur kuno; siapa pun yang mendekat pada malam musim panas mungkin mendengar musik indah tetapi harus waspada agar tidak terjebak. Sebagian baik hati, menyembuhkan penyakit dan menolong dari kemalangan; sebagian lain jahat, mencuri anak dan membawa sial. Dahulu orang menyalakan api semalaman, meninggalkan roti dan mengisi kendi dengan air bersih untuk mereka, dan perempuan Manx tidak memintal pada Sabtu malam. Garam dan besi dianggap ampuh melawan pesona jahat.",
      "en": "They live on green hillsides, especially ancient burial mounds; anyone lingering near one on a summer evening might hear lovely music but must beware of being trapped. Some are kindly, curing illness and rescuing people from misfortune; others are wicked, stealing children and bringing bad luck. People used to keep a fire burning overnight, leave bread and fill water crocks for them, and Manx women would not spin on Saturday evenings. Salt and iron were thought effective against evil charms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mooinjer-veggey-c03",
        "mooinjer-veggey-c04",
        "mooinjer-veggey-c05"
      ]
    }

      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",

        "id": "Setinggi 2–3 kaki, topi merah, jaket hijau, menunggang kuda dengan anjing kecil warna pelangi; nakal dan pendendam.",

      "id": "mooinjer-veggey-c03",
      "source_id": "mooinjer-veggey-s1",
      "quote": "They live in green hillsides, more especially ancient tumuli. Anyone straying near these on a fine summer's evening would probably hear delightful music",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tinggal di lereng hijau dan gundukan kubur kuno; musik indah malam musim panas.",
        "en": "Live on green hillsides and ancient tumuli; delightful music on summer evenings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mooinjer-veggey-c04",
      "source_id": "mooinjer-veggey-s1",
      "quote": "They are visible to people only when they choose. Some of them are benevolent, curing men of diseases and delivering them from misfortune. Others are malevolent, stealing children, even abducting adults, and bringing misfortune.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terlihat hanya bila mau; sebagian menyembuhkan, sebagian mencuri anak.",
        "en": "Visible only when they choose; some heal, some steal children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mooinjer-veggey-c05",
      "source_id": "mooinjer-veggey-s1",
      "quote": "Manxwomen would not spin on Saturday evenings, as this was deemed displeasing to the mooinjer veggey , and at every baking and churning a small piece of dough and butter was stuck on the wall for their consumption.       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perempuan Manx (Manxwomen) tak memintal Sabtu malam; api, roti, air untuk mereka; garam dan besi melawan pesona jahat.",

    },
    {
      "id": "mooinjer-veggey-c06",
      "source_id": "mooinjer-veggey-s2",

    {

      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Pillywiggin adalah goblin dan peri mungil penjaga tumbuhan dalam folklor Inggris dan Irlandia, bersayap kupu-kupu atau capung.",
    "en": "Pillywiggins are tiny goblins and fairies who guard plant life in English and Irish folklore, with butterfly or dragonfly wings.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "pillywiggin-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Pillywiggin adalah goblin dan peri mungil penjaga tumbuhan yang disebut dalam folklor Inggris dan Irlandia. Bertubuh sangat kecil, mereka memiliki antena dan sayap kupu-kupu atau capung, hidup berkelompok, dan menghabiskan waktu bermain di antara bunga. Mereka dikaitkan dengan bunga musim semi dan mempersonifikasikan \"esensi ilahi tumbuhan\". Nama \"Pillywiggin\" muncul pada 1977 dalam Field Guide to the Little People karya Nancy Arrowsmith, yang meyakini nama itu berasal dari county Dorset.",
      "en": "Pillywiggins are tiny goblins and fairies, keepers of the flowers, found in English and Irish folklore. Minute in size, with antennae and with wings like those of butterflies or dragonflies, they keep together in bands and spend their days at play among the blossoms. Linked with spring flowers, they embody the 'divine essence of plants'. The name 'Pillywiggin' appeared in 1977 in Nancy Arrowsmith's Field Guide to the Little People, which traces it to the county of Dorset.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pillywiggin-c01",

    {
      "id": "Mereka biasanya berukuran satu sentimeter tetapi dapat berubah ukuran, dan makan embun serta serbuk sari. Sebagai peri berkelompok, mereka tak terlalu peduli pada manusia tetapi kadang ikut serta dalam pesta seperti pernikahan; tidak seperti peri Inggris lain, mereka tidak dikenal suka mengerjai manusia. Edain McCoy (1994) menggambarkan ratu pillywiggin bernama Ariel yang menunggang kelelawar.",
      "en": "They usually measure about a centimetre but can change size, and live on dew and pollen. As trooping fairies they take little interest in people, though they may join in weddings and other festivities; unlike many other fairies they are not known for playing tricks. Edain McCoy (1994) describes a pillywiggin queen named Ariel who rides bats.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Seelie adalah istilah Skots yang berarti \"bahagia, beruntung, diberkati\" untuk makhluk peri dalam folklor Skotlandia, seperti dalam Seelie Court.",
    "en": "Seelie is a Scots word meaning 'happy, lucky, blessed', applied to fairy beings in Scottish folklore, as in the Seelie Court.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "seelie-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Seelie (atau Seely) berasal dari kata Skots seelie yang berarti \"bahagia\", \"beruntung\" atau \"diberkati\", dipakai untuk makhluk peri dalam folklor Skotlandia, misalnya seely wights atau Seelie Court. Meski namanya demikian, makhluk seelie dalam legenda bisa ambivalen secara moral dan berbahaya; menyebut mereka \"seelie\", seperti sebutan \"tetangga baik\", mungkin eufemisme untuk menghindari amarah mereka. Kata ini berasal dari Skots Kuno sele, \"keberuntungan, kebahagiaan\", dan kata Inggris modern \"silly\" juga turunan dari \"seely\".",
      "en": "Seelie (or Seely) comes from the Scots seelie, 'happy', 'lucky' or 'blessed', and Scottish folklore uses it of fairy beings, as in 'seely wights' or 'the Seelie Court'. For all the name, seelie folk in legend could be morally ambivalent and dangerous; calling them 'seelie', like 'good neighbours', may have been a euphemism to avoid their anger. The word goes back to Old Scots sele, 'good fortune, bliss', and modern English 'silly' descends from 'seely' too.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seelie-c01",
        "seelie-c02",
        "seelie-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Banyak balada dan kisah Skotlandia menyebut \"Seilie wichts\", makhluk yang diberkati; Julian Goodare menganggap mereka roh alam yang berbeda dari peri, dan William Hay pada abad ke-16 mengeluhkan penyihir yang mengaku bertemu perempuan mirip peri bernama \"celly vichtys\". Seelie Court adalah kelompok peri baik yang berlawanan dengan Unseelie Court yang jahat; menurut Katharine Mary Briggs, mereka meminta bantuan manusia dan membalas kebaikan, tetapi tetap membalas penghinaan. Artikel cs menyebut mereka sering muncul saat senja dalam arak-arakan yang anggun.",
      "en": "Many Scottish ballads and tales speak of 'Seilie wichts', blessed beings; Julian Goodare took them for nature spirits distinct from fairies, and in the 16th century William Hay complained of witches claiming to meet fairy-like women called 'celly vichtys'. The Seelie Court is a band of good fairies set against the wicked Unseelie Court; Katharine Mary Briggs says they seek human help and repay kindness, yet still avenge insults. The cs article adds that they often appear at dusk in elegant processions.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "seelie-c04",
        "seelie-c05",
        "seelie-c06"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shellycoat adalah roh air jahil dalam folklor Skotlandia dan Inggris Utara yang menghantui sungai dan aliran air, berselimut cangkang.",
    "en": "A shellycoat is a prankster water spirit of Scottish and Northern English folklore that haunts rivers and streams, covered in shells.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "shellycoat-c01",
      "shellycoat-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Skotlandia dan Inggris Utara, shellycoat (juga spellycoat) adalah roh air jahil yang menghantui sungai dan aliran air, terutama di pesisir timur Skotlandia. Tubuhnya berbulu panjang dan berkaki selaput; bulunya tertutup cangkang dan sampah laut yang berderak saat ia bergerak. Ia relatif tak berbahaya dibanding kelpie: ia menyesatkan pengembara tanpa niat jahat, sering berteriak seperti orang tenggelam lalu tertawa seperti kuda.",
      "en": "In Scottish and Northern English folklore the shellycoat (also spellycoat) is a mischievous water spirit haunting rivers and streams, mostly on Scotland's east coast. It has long hair over its whole body and webbed feet, the hair hung with shells and sea debris that clatter as it moves. Compared with the kelpie it is fairly harmless: it leads wanderers astray without malice, often crying out as if drowning and then laughing like a horse.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shellycoat-c01",
        "shellycoat-c02",
        "shellycoat-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Shellycoat konon tak dapat menyeberangi air mengalir dan membiarkan perempuan yang membawa anak. Shellycoat dari Leith digambarkan roh jahat besar yang dapat terbang, menyembunyikan mantel cangkangnya di bawah batu; seorang bernama English Dick yang menantangnya ditemukan dengan kaki patah. Batunya hancur saat pembangunan dermaga awal 1900-an. Jacob Grimm dalam Deutsche Mythologie menyamakannya dengan Schellenrock Jerman, \"mantel lonceng\".",
      "en": "It is believed unable to cross running water and to leave women with children alone. The shellycoat of Leith was described as a large, evil spirit able to fly, hiding its magic coat of shells under a rock; a man called English Dick who challenged it was found with broken legs. Its rock was destroyed in dock building in the early 1900s. Jacob Grimm in his Deutsche Mythologie equated it with the German Schellenrock, 'bell-coat'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shellycoat-c04",
        "shellycoat-c05",
        "shellycoat-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "etymology": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shellycoat-c01",
      "source_id": "shellycoat-s1",
      "quote": "In Scottish and Northern English folklore, a shellycoat (also called spellycoat) is a prankster water spirit that haunts rivers and streams. They are mainly described on the east coast of Scotland.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shellycoat (spellycoat), roh air jahil folklor Skotlandia (Scottish, Scotland) dan Inggris Utara (Northern English); sungai; pesisir timur.",
        "en": "Shellycoat (spellycoat), a prankster water spirit of Scottish and Northern English folklore; rivers; east coast."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shellycoat-c02",
      "source_id": "shellycoat-s1",
      "quote": "The shellycoat is said to have long hair all over its body, along with webbed feet. The hair is covered in shells and other ocean debris, making a clattering sound as it moves. They are similar to a tangie, which is covered in seaweed.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bulu panjang, kaki berselaput, tertutup cangkang yang berderak; mirip tangie.",
        "en": "Long hair, webbed feet, covered in clattering shells; like a tangie."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shellycoat-c03",
      "source_id": "shellycoat-s1",
      "quote": "Shellycoats are considered to be relatively harmless compared to other water spirits like the kelpie. They may mislead wanderers, particularly those they think are trespassing upon the creature's territory, but without malice. A common tactic of a shellycoat would be to cry out as if drowning and then laugh like a horse at the distracted victim.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tak berbahaya dibanding kelpie; menyesatkan tanpa niat jahat; berteriak seperti tenggelam lalu tertawa seperti kuda.",
        "en": "Harmless compared to the kelpie; misleads without malice; cries as if drowning, laughs like a horse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shellycoat-c04",
      "source_id": "shellycoat-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed that the shellycoat cannot cross running water and that it leaves women with children alone.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tak dapat menyeberangi air mengalir; membiarkan perempuan beranak.",
        "en": "Cannot cross running water; leaves women with children alone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shellycoat-c05",
      "source_id": "shellycoat-s1",
      "quote": "The shellycoat of Leith was described as a large, evil spirit that was capable of flight. He would hide his magic coat of shells under a rock when not wearing it, during which times he was harmless.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shellycoat dari (of) Leith: roh jahat besar, dapat terbang; English Dick; batu hancur awal 1900-an.",
        "en": "Shellycoat of Leith: large evil spirit, could fly; English Dick; rock destroyed early 1900s."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shellycoat-c06",
      "source_id": "shellycoat-s1",
      "quote": "Jacob Grimm stated in his Deutsche Mythologie that the Scottish goblin Shellycoat is one and the same as the German Schellenrock, that is bell-coat",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jacob Grimm, Deutsche Mythologie: sama dengan Schellenrock Jerman (German), mantel lonceng.",
        "en": "Jacob Grimm, Deutsche Mythologie: same as the German Schellenrock, bell-coat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shellycoat-c07",
      "source_id": "shellycoat-s2",
      "quote": "Das Klappern der Muscheln verrät dem aufmerksamen Wanderer seine Anwesenheit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel de: derak cangkang menandakan kehadirannya.",
        "en": "de article: the clatter of shells reveals its presence."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Stoor worm adalah ular laut raksasa yang jahat dalam folklor Orkney, yang napas busuknya meracuni tumbuhan dan membunuh hewan serta manusia.",
    "en": "The stoor worm is a gigantic evil sea serpent of Orcadian folklore whose putrid breath blights plants and kills animals and people.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "stoor-worm-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Stoor worm atau Mester Stoor Worm adalah ular laut raksasa yang jahat dalam folklor Orkney, yang mampu meracuni tumbuhan dan membinasakan hewan serta manusia dengan napas busuknya. Ia kemungkinan varian Orkney dari Jörmungandr Norse, Ular Midgard, dan digambarkan sebagai naga laut. Namanya mungkin berasal dari Norse Kuno Storðar-gandr, nama lain Jörmungandr; \"Mester\" berarti tuan, karena ia \"tuan dan bapak semua stoorworm\".",
      "en": "The stoor worm, or Mester Stoor Worm, is a gigantic wicked sea serpent of Orkney folklore that could blight plants and kill animals and people with its rotten breath. It is probably an Orkney form of the Norse Jörmungandr, the Midgard Serpent, and has been called a sea dragon. The name may derive from Old Norse Storðar-gandr, another name for Jörmungandr, and 'Mester' means master, as it was the 'master and father of all stoorworms'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "stoor-worm-c01",
        "stoor-worm-c03",
        "stoor-worm-c04",
        "stoor-worm-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang raja yang negerinya terancam dinasihati mempersembahkan tujuh perawan setiap minggu; akhirnya ia menawarkan kerajaan, tangan putrinya dan pedang ajaib kepada siapa pun yang dapat membinasakan monster itu. Assipattle, putra bungsu seorang petani, mengalahkannya; saat ia mati, giginya rontok menjadi Kepulauan Orkney, Shetland dan Faroe, dan tubuhnya menjadi Islandia.",
      "en": "A king whose land it threatened was advised to feed it seven virgins every week; in the end he offered his kingdom, his daughter's hand and a magic sword to whoever could destroy it. Assipattle, a farmer's youngest son, overcame it; as it died its teeth dropped out to form Orkney, Shetland and the Faroes, and its body became Iceland.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Laidly Worm dari Spindleston Heugh adalah balada Northumbria tentang seorang putri yang diubah menjadi naga oleh ibu tirinya yang penyihir.",
    "en": "The Laidly Worm of Spindleston Heugh is a Northumbrian ballad about a princess turned into a dragon by her witch stepmother.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c01",
      "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "The Laidly Worm of Spindleston Heugh, juga The Laidly Worm of Bamborough, adalah balada Northumbria tentang seorang putri yang diubah menjadi naga, \"laidly worm\" (naga buruk rupa). Di Kerajaan Northumbria, seorang raja baik di Kastel Bamburgh menikahi penyihir cantik tetapi kejam setelah istrinya wafat. Saat putranya Childe Wynd berada di seberang laut, penyihir itu, iri pada kecantikan Putri Margaret, mengubahnya menjadi naga.",
      "en": "The Laidly Worm of Spindleston Heugh, also The Laidly Worm of Bamborough, is a Northumbrian ballad of a princess changed into a dragon, the 'laidly worm' (loathsome serpent) of the title. In the Kingdom of Northumbria a good king at Bamburgh Castle weds a lovely but cruel witch after his wife dies. While his son Childe Wynd is overseas, the witch, envious of Princess Margaret's beauty, turns her into a dragon.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c01",
        "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c02",
        "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sang pangeran pulang dan, alih-alih melawan naga itu, menciumnya tiga kali, sehingga sang putri kembali ke wujud aslinya; ia lalu mengubah ratu penyihir menjadi katak dan menjadi raja. Balada ini pertama diterbitkan pada 1778, konon disalin Pendeta Robert Lambe dari naskah Latin tahun 1270, tetapi kini dianggap pemalsuan Lambe yang memakai kisah setempat. Dalam versi Joseph Jacobs, naga itu ditenangkan dengan tujuh sapi per hari.",
      "en": "The prince returns and, rather than fighting the dragon, kisses it three times, and the princess regains her own shape; afterwards he changes the witch-queen into a toad and takes the throne. The ballad was first printed in 1778, supposedly copied by the Reverend Robert Lambe from a Latin manuscript of 1270, but is now thought to be Lambe's forgery drawing on local tales. In Joseph Jacobs's version the dragon is pacified with seven cows a day.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c03",
        "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c04",

      "id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c01",
      "source_id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-s1",
      "quote": "The Laidly Worm of Spindleston Heugh, also known as The Laidly Worm of Bamborough, is a Northumbrian ballad about a princess who is changed into a dragon (the \"laidly worm\" of the title).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Laidly Worm dari (of) Spindleston Heugh (The Laidly Worm of Bamborough), balada Northumbria (Northumbrian); putri diubah menjadi naga.",
        "en": "The Laidly Worm of Spindleston Heugh (of Bamborough), a Northumbrian ballad; a princess turned into a dragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c02",
      "source_id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-s1",
      "quote": "The King's son, Childe Wynd, has gone across the sea and the witch, jealous of the beauty of the king’s daughter, Princess Margaret, and quick to take advantage of Wynd’s absence, turns her into a dragon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kerajaan (Kingdom of) Northumbria, Kastel (Castle) Bamburgh; penyihir iri pada Putri (Princess) Margaret mengubahnya jadi naga; Childe Wynd di seberang laut.",
        "en": "Kingdom of Northumbria, Bamburgh Castle; the witch, jealous of Princess Margaret, turns her into a dragon; Childe Wynd overseas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c03",
      "source_id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-s1",
      "quote": "Later in the story, the prince returns and, instead of fighting the dragon, kisses it, restoring the princess to her natural form. He then turns the witch-queen into a toad and becomes king.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pangeran mencium naga tiga kali, putri pulih; ratu penyihir menjadi katak.",
        "en": "The prince kisses the dragon thrice, the princess restored; the witch-queen made a toad."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-c04",
      "source_id": "the-laidly-worm-of-spindleston-heugh-s1",
      "quote": "The ballad was first published in 1778 in a compilation of folk songs.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Diterbitkan pertama 1778; Pendeta Robert Lambe; naskah Latin 1270; dianggap pemalsuan.",

    {

      "quote": "In Joseph Jacob's version, the dragon Princess Margaret is appeased by putting aside seven cows for her per day.",

        "id": "Versi Joseph Jacobs: tujuh sapi per hari.",

      "quote": "ある日七王国時代の国の1つであるノーサンブリア王国のバンバラ城（Bamburgh Castle）の王が新しい后（女王）を連れて戻ってきた。",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ratman dari Southend adalah legenda urban Inggris dari Southend-on-Sea, Essex, tentang hantu lelaki tunawisma yang wajahnya dimakan tikus.",
    "en": "The Ratman of Southend is an English urban legend from Southend-on-Sea, Essex, about the ghost of a homeless man whose face was eaten by rats.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "the-ratman-of-southend-c01",
      "the-ratman-of-southend-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ratman dari Southend adalah legenda urban Inggris yang berasal dari Southend-on-Sea, Essex. Kisahnya tentang seorang lelaki tua tunawisma yang, saat berlindung dari dingin di sebuah terowongan bawah jalan, diserang sekelompok pemuda dan dipukuli hingga hampir mati; dingin dan kehilangan darah mengakhiri hidupnya. Saat ia mati, binatang pengerat yang menghuni tempat itu berkumpul dan ternyata memakan wajahnya.",
      "en": "The Ratman of Southend is an English urban legend from Southend-on-Sea in Essex. It tells of an old homeless man who, sheltering from the cold in an underpass, was attacked by a gang of youths and beaten nearly to death; cold and blood loss did the rest. As he died the vermin of the place gathered and were found to have eaten his face.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-ratman-of-southend-c01",
        "the-ratman-of-southend-c02",
        "the-ratman-of-southend-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setelah itu sesosok bayangan hantu terlihat di terowongan itu, dan orang mendengar cicitan seperti tikus serta bunyi garukan seolah cakar besar bergerak di dinding. Sebuah film pendek tentang Ratman karya Michael Holiday diikutkan dalam festival Horror-on-Sea pada 2019, dan Ratman muncul sebagai musuh dalam mod Fallout: London (2024).",
      "en": "Afterwards a ghostly figure was seen in the underpass, with rat-like squeals and scraping as of large claws on the walls. A short film about the Ratman by Michael Holiday was entered in the 2019 Horror-on-Sea festival, and the Ratman appears as an enemy in the 2024 mod Fallout: London.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tréo-Fall (danserien-noz) adalah makhluk mirip lutin dari folklor Basse-Bretagne, khas Pulau Ouessant, yang menari di atas tebing di bawah cahaya bulan.",
    "en": "The Tréo-Fall (danserien-noz) are lutin-like beings of Lower Brittany folklore, peculiar to Ushant, who dance on clifftops by moonlight.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "treo-fall-c01",
      "treo-fall-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tréo-Fall, juga dikenal sebagai danserien-noz (\"penari malam\" dalam bahasa Breton), adalah makhluk mirip lutin dari folklor Basse-Bretagne. Dengan nama pertama, mereka khas Pulau Ouessant, tempat tradisinya dikumpulkan François-Marie Luzel. Mereka berkumpul di puncak tebing untuk menari di bawah cahaya bulan dan menjanjikan harta kepada manusia yang lewat; dua kisah tentang \"penari malam\" dikumpulkan di Plouaret pada Januari 1869.",
      "en": "The Tréo-Fall, also known as danserien-noz ('night dancers' in Breton), are lutin-like beings of Lower Brittany folklore. Under the first name they belong only to the island of Ushant, where François-Marie Luzel gathered their lore. They meet on clifftops to dance in the moonlight, promising treasure to people who cross their path; two tales of 'night dancers' were recorded at Plouaret in January 1869.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "treo-fall-c01",
        "treo-fall-c02",
        "treo-fall-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Paul Sébillot, gambaran mereka samar tetapi menekankan tubuh yang kecil. Mereka mengajak manusia ikut menari dengan janji harta, padahal itu jebakan: para penari sering berakhir dengan ginjal rusak. Satu-satunya jalan keluar adalah menancapkan pisau di tanah dan menyentuhnya di setiap putaran tarian; bila berhasil, Tréo-Fall akan mengabulkan permintaan apa pun. Mereka memberi hadiah kepada yang ikut menari dan menghukum yang menghina.",
      "en": "Paul Sébillot says descriptions are vague but stress their small size. They invite people into their dance with promises of treasure, but it is a trap and dancers often end up with broken kidneys. The only escape is to stick a knife in the ground and touch it at each round of the dance; whoever manages this has any wish granted. They reward those who join the dance and punish those who insult them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "treo-fall-c03",
        "treo-fall-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "treo-fall-c01",
      "source_id": "treo-fall-s1",
      "quote": "The Tréo-Fall, also known as danserien-noz, are lutin-like creatures from the folklore of Lower Brittany. Under this first name, they are specific to the island of Ushant, where traditions about them were collected by François-Marie Luzel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tréo-Fall (danserien-noz), mirip lutin, folklor Basse-Bretagne (Lower Brittany, Prancis); Pulau Ouessant (Ushant); François-Marie Luzel; Breton.",
        "en": "Tréo-Fall (danserien-noz), lutin-like, Lower Brittany folklore; island of Ushant; François-Marie Luzel; Breton."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treo-fall-c02",
      "source_id": "treo-fall-s1",
      "quote": "The Tréo-Fall are said to gather at the top of cliffs to dance in the moonlight, promising treasures to humans who cross their path. Two tales about \"night dancers\" were collected in Plouaret in January 1869.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menari di puncak tebing di bawah bulan, menjanjikan harta; Plouaret, Januari (January) 1869.",
        "en": "Dance on clifftops by moonlight, promising treasure; Plouaret, January 1869."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treo-fall-c03",
      "source_id": "treo-fall-s1",
      "quote": "The only way to escape is to stick a knife in the ground, then graze it at each dance circle. If the dancer succeeds in fulfilling this condition, the Tréo-Fall will grant his or her request, whatever it may be.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pisau di tanah disentuh tiap putaran; permintaan dikabulkan; penari sering ginjal rusak; Paul Sébillot: tubuh kecil.",
        "en": "A knife in the ground touched each round; wish granted; dancers often break kidneys; Paul Sébillot: small size."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treo-fall-c04",
      "source_id": "treo-fall-s1",
      "quote": "François-Marie Luzel and Françoise Morvan see the Tréo-Fall as a particular variety of Korrigans, linked to a local use of the knife on the island of Ouessant.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "François-Marie Luzel dan Françoise Morvan: jenis Korrigan; pisau di Ouessant; penari malam (danseur de nuit); hadiah dan hukuman.",
        "en": "François-Marie Luzel and Françoise Morvan: a kind of Korrigan; the knife on Ouessant; night dancer (danseur de nuit); reward and punishment."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "treo-fall-c05",
      "source_id": "treo-fall-s2",
      "quote": "Les Tréo-Fall, également connus sous le nom de danserien-noz, sont des créatures du folklore populaire de la Basse-Bretagne, de type lutin.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ysgithyrwyn, \"Si Taring Putih kepala para babi hutan\", adalah babi hutan yang diburu rombongan Arthur dalam roman Welsh Culhwch ac Olwen.",
    "en": "Ysgithyrwyn, 'White-tusk chief of Boars', is a boar hunted by Arthur's band in the Welsh romance Culhwch ac Olwen.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ysgithyrwyn Penbaedd, atau \"Si Taring Putih kepala para babi hutan\", adalah babi hutan lain yang diburu rombongan Arthur dalam roman Arthurian Welsh Culhwch ac Olwen, di samping babi hutan besar Twrch Trwyth. Taringnya (Welsh: ysgithyr) adalah alat yang diperlukan untuk mencukur raksasa Ysbaddaden, kepala para raksasa. Ysbaddaden menyatakan taring itu tak berguna kecuali dicabut selagi babi itu masih hidup, dan hanya Odgar putra Aedd, raja Irlandia, yang mampu melakukannya.",
      "en": "Ysgithyrwyn Penbaedd, the 'White-tusk chief of Boars', is a second boar hunted by Arthur's party in the Welsh Arthurian romance Culhwch ac Olwen, alongside the great boar Twrch Trwyth. Its tusk (Welsh ysgithyr) was the tool needed to shave the giant Ysbaddaden, chief of giants. Ysbaddaden declared the tusk useless unless pulled from the boar while it still lived, and only Odgar son of Aedd, king of Ireland, could do this.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ysgithyrwyn-c01",
        "ysgithyrwyn-c02",
        "ysgithyrwyn-c05",
        "ysgithyrwyn-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Babi itu dibunuh, atau setidaknya dikejar hingga terpojok, bukan oleh anjing-anjing yang disebut Ysbaddaden melainkan oleh Cavall, anjing Arthur sendiri. Sebenarnya Kaw dari Britania Utara, menunggang kuda betina Arthur Llamrei, mengayunkan kapak dan membelah kepala babi itu, lalu mengambil taringnya sesuai sumpah Arthur. Artikel de menyebut babi dan babi jantan sebagai hewan penting bagi bangsa Kelt dan sering memicu perang dalam Mabinogion.",
      "en": "The boar was killed, or at least run down and cornered, not by the dogs Ysbaddaden had named but by Cavall, Arthur's own hound. In fact Kaw of North Britain, riding Arthur's mare Llamrei, swung an axe and split its head, then took charge of the tusk as Arthur had vowed. The de article notes that pigs and boars were key animals for the Celts and often set off wars in the Mabinogion.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ysgithyrwyn-c03",
        "ysgithyrwyn-c04",
        "ysgithyrwyn-c07"
      ]

      "source_id": "ysgithyrwyn-s2",
      "quote": "Ysgithyrwyn Penbaedd [ ɘsgi'θɘruin 'penbaið] („Weißhauer der oberste Eber“), walisisch Ysgithrwyn Pen Beidd, Yskithyrwynn Pennbeidd; mittelwalisische yskithyrwyn penn beird, ist der Name eines mythischen Ebers in der Walisischen Mythologie im Sagenkreis um König Arthur.",

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Adar Rhiannon (\"burung-burung Rhiannon\") adalah tiga burung ajaib dalam Mabinogi yang nyanyiannya dapat membangunkan orang mati dan menidurkan yang hidup.",
    "en": "The Adar Rhiannon ('birds of Rhiannon') are three magic birds of the Mabinogi, able with their song to rouse the dead and send the living to sleep.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "adar-rhiannon-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita prosa tertua Britania, Mabinogi, Adar Rhiannon atau \"burung-burung Rhiannon\" adalah tiga burung ajaib yang nyanyiannya dapat \"membangunkan orang mati dan menidurkan yang hidup\". Mereka juga memengaruhi ruang secara ganjil: bisa berada jauh tetapi terdengar sangat dekat. Mereka dikaitkan dengan Rhiannon, Ratu Dyfed, yang dianggap dewi kuda Britania yang telah dimanusiakan.",
      "en": "In Britain's oldest prose tales, the Mabinogi, the Adar Rhiannon or 'birds of Rhiannon' are three magic birds whose singing is said to raise the dead and put the living to sleep. They bend space strangely, seeming close at hand while far away. They are tied to Rhiannon, Queen of Dyfed, thought to be a British horse goddess made human in the tales.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adar-rhiannon-c01",
        "adar-rhiannon-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Culhwch and Olwen, raksasa Ysbaddaden Bencawr menuntut burung-burung itu sebagai salah satu tugas pernikahan Culhwch, agar menghiburnya pada malam terakhir hidupnya. Dalam cabang kedua Mabinogi, Branwen ferch Llŷr, tujuh orang yang selamat dari perang dengan Irlandia berpesta di Harlech selama tujuh tahun ajaib sambil dihibur tiga burung Rhiannon; terjemahan Lady Guest menyebut burung-burung itu tampak jauh di atas laut tetapi terdengar jelas seolah dekat.",
      "en": "In Culhwch and Olwen the giant Ysbaddaden Bencawr demands the birds as one of Culhwch's bridal tasks, to soothe him on the last night of his life. In the Mabinogi's second branch, Branwen ferch Llŷr, the seven survivors of the war with Ireland feast at Harlech for seven enchanted years to the song of the three birds; Lady Guest's translation says they seemed far off over the sea yet sounded as clear as if close by.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adar-rhiannon-c03",
        "adar-rhiannon-c04",
        "adar-rhiannon-c05",
        "adar-rhiannon-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "adar-rhiannon-c01",
      "source_id": "adar-rhiannon-s1",
      "quote": "the Adar Rhiannon ; \"birds of Rhiannon\", are specifically three magical birds, whose song can \"wake the dead and lull the living to sleep\". They also have a non-rational effect on space as they can be remote but seem very near.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Adar Rhiannon (burung-burung Rhiannon), tiga burung ajaib Mabinogi; membangunkan orang mati, menidurkan yang hidup; jauh tetapi tampak dekat; Britania.",
        "en": "Adar Rhiannon (birds of Rhiannon), three magical birds of the Mabinogi; wake the dead, lull the living; remote yet near; Britain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adar-rhiannon-c02",
      "source_id": "adar-rhiannon-s1",
      "quote": "They are connected with Rhiannon the Queen of Dyfed who is thought to be a British euhemerized horse goddess, so part of Welsh mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terkait Rhiannon, Ratu (Queen of) Dyfed, dewi kuda Britania (British); mitologi Welsh, Wales.",
        "en": "Connected with Rhiannon, Queen of Dyfed, a British horse goddess; Welsh mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adar-rhiannon-c03",
      "source_id": "adar-rhiannon-s1",
      "quote": "The Adar Rhiannon were demanded by the giant Ysbaddaden Bencawr as a marriage task for Culhwch to complete. The giant wanted the Birds to soothe him as he faced his last night of life, prophesied as the consequence of his daughter's wedding.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ysbaddaden Bencawr menuntutnya sebagai tugas Culhwch (Culhwch and Olwen); malam terakhir hidupnya.",
        "en": "Ysbaddaden Bencawr demanded them as Culhwch's task (Culhwch and Olwen); his last night of life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adar-rhiannon-c04",
      "source_id": "adar-rhiannon-s1",
      "quote": "Before setting off, the Seven Survivors feast at Harlech for seven enchanted years, regaled by the three Birds of Rhiannon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Branwen ferch Llŷr: tujuh penyintas berpesta di Harlech tujuh tahun; perang dengan Irlandia.",
        "en": "Branwen ferch Llŷr: seven survivors feast at Harlech seven years; war with Ireland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adar-rhiannon-c05",
      "source_id": "adar-rhiannon-s2",
      "quote": "In Harlech you will be feasting seven years, the birds of Rhiannon singing unto you the while.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Lady Guest: tujuh tahun berpesta di Harlech diiringi nyanyian burung Rhiannon.",
        "en": "Lady Guest translation: seven years feasting at Harlech with the birds of Rhiannon singing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adar-rhiannon-c06",
      "source_id": "adar-rhiannon-s2",
      "quote": "and the birds seemed to them to be at a great distance from them over the sea, yet they appeared as distinct as if they were close by",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Adhene adalah sebutan untuk peri dalam tradisi Manx, juga disebut Cloan ny moyrn, \"Anak-anak Kesombongan\", karena dianggap malaikat yang jatuh.",
    "en": "Adhene is the Manx name for fairies, also called Cloan ny moyrn, 'Children of Pride', as they were held to be fallen angels.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "adhene-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi Manx, peri disebut Adhene dan dikenal sebagai Cloan ny moyrn, yang berarti \"Anak-anak Kesombongan\", karena dianggap malaikat yang terusir dari surga tetapi terlalu baik untuk neraka. Mereka kadang baik hati, tetapi umumnya nakal terhadap manusia, mengambil bayi atau istri sesuka hati, meski kekuatan mereka diyakini tak berlaku atas orang yang sedang menjalankan tugas belas kasih.",
      "en": "The Manx name for fairies was Adhene, and they were also known as Cloan ny moyrn, 'the Children of Pride (or Ambition)', since they were thought to be angels cast out of heaven but too good for hell. They could be kindly but were mostly mischievous towards people, carrying off babies or wives at will, though their power was believed useless against anyone on an errand of mercy.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adhene-c01",
        "adhene-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bila terlihat, ukuran mereka kira-kira seperti anak kecil; mereka memancing di laut dan menggembalakan ternak di perbukitan. Orang Manx tahu akan ada tangkapan ikan atau panen yang baik bila mendengar para peri membuat tong penyimpanan di gua-gua.",
      "en": "When visible they were about the size of a small child; they fished at sea and herded cattle on the hills. Manx people knew a good fishing season or harvest was coming when they heard the fairies making storage barrels in the caves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adhene-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "In Manx tradition, fairies were called Adhene and known as Cloan ny moyrn , which means the Children of Pride/Ambition, because they were regarded as having been fallen angels cast from heaven but too good for hell.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Adhene, peri tradisi Manx, Pulau Man; Cloan ny moyrn (Anak-anak Kesombongan, Children of Pride); malaikat jatuh.",
        "en": "Adhene, fairies of Manx tradition, Isle of Man; Cloan ny moyrn (Children of Pride); fallen angels."

      "source_id": "adhene-s1",
      "quote": "They could be benevolent but were mostly mischievous in association with humans, taking babies or wives when they wished, although it was believed that their powers were not effective over any human on an errand of mercy.",

    "id": "Ascapart adalah raksasa legenda dalam folklor Inggris yang ditaklukkan Bevis dari Hampton, cukup besar untuk mengepit Bevis, istrinya dan kudanya.",
    "en": "Ascapart is a legendary giant of English folklore conquered by Bevis of Hampton, big enough to carry Bevis, his wife and his horse under one arm.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ascapart-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Inggris, Ascapart (juga Ascupart, Ascapard, Ascopard, Ascopart, Asgapard) adalah raksasa legenda yang konon ditaklukkan Bevis dari Hampton, begitu besar hingga dapat mengepit Bevis, istrinya dan kudanya. Tingginya 30 kaki tetapi ia yang terkecil di negerinya. Ia kalah setelah gadanya, sebatang pohon utuh, diayunkan ke arah Sir Bevis dan tertancap di tanah lunak; Bevis tidak membunuhnya melainkan menjadikannya pengawal.",
      "en": "In English folklore Ascapart (also Ascupart, Ascapard, Ascopard, Ascopart, Asgapard) is a legendary giant said to have been overcome by Bevis of Hampton, so huge he could carry Bevis, his wife and his horse under his arm. He was 30 feet tall, yet the smallest in his land. He lost when his club, a whole tree, was swung at Sir Bevis and stuck fast in soft ground; Bevis spared him and made him his squire.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ascapart-c01",
        "ascapart-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kemudian Ascapart mengkhianati Bevis dan membawa lari istrinya Josiane, lalu dibunuh sahabat-sahabat Bevis. Ia konon dikubur di bawah Bevis Mount, atau tubuhnya menjadi bagian South Downs saat ia jatuh. Raksasa ini juga muncul dalam folklor Hampshire dan New Forest; panel lukisan abad pertengahan Sir Bevis dan Ascupart ada di Southampton Bargate Monument Gallery.",
      "en": "Later Ascapart betrayed Bevis and carried off his wife Josiane, and Bevis's friends killed him. He is said to lie under Bevis Mount, or his body became part of the South Downs when he fell. The giant also figures in the folklore of Hampshire and the New Forest; medieval painted panels of Sir Bevis and Ascupart hang in the Southampton Bargate Monument Gallery.",

        "ascapart-c03",
        "ascapart-c04",
        "ascapart-c05"

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ascapart-c01",
      "source_id": "ascapart-s1",
      "quote": "In English folklore, Ascapart (also spelled Ascupart, Ascapard, Ascopard, Ascopart and Asgapard) was a legendary giant supposedly conquered by Bevis of Hampton, so huge as to carry Bevis, his wife, and horse under his arm.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ascapart (Ascupart, Ascapard, Ascopard, Ascopart, Asgapard), raksasa folklor Inggris (English), ditaklukkan Bevis dari (of) Hampton; mengepit Bevis, istri, kuda.",
        "en": "Ascapart (Ascupart, Ascapard, Ascopard, Ascopart, Asgapard), giant of English folklore conquered by Bevis of Hampton; carried Bevis, wife, horse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ascapart-c02",
      "source_id": "ascapart-s1",
      "quote": "He was 30 feet tall but the smallest of his land, and was defeated after his club (made from a whole tree) was swung at Sir Bevis and became stuck in soft ground. Rather than slaying the giant however, Sir Bevis decided to make him his Squire.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "30 kaki, terkecil di negerinya; gada pohon tertancap; Sir Bevis menjadikannya pengawal.",
        "en": "30 feet, smallest of his land; tree club stuck; Sir Bevis made him squire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ascapart-c03",
      "source_id": "ascapart-s1",
      "quote": "Later Ascapart betrayed Bevis and took his wife Josiane, who was imprisoned with Ascapart as her jailer. For this Ascapart was killed by Bevis' friends when they freed Josiane.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mengkhianati Bevis, membawa Josiane; dibunuh sahabat Bevis.",
        "en": "Betrayed Bevis, took Josiane; killed by Bevis's friends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ascapart-c04",
      "source_id": "ascapart-s1",
      "quote": "Ascapart was supposedly buried under Bevis Mount, or his body became a part of the South Downs when he fell.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dikubur di Bevis Mount atau menjadi bagian South Downs.",
        "en": "Buried under Bevis Mount or became part of the South Downs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ascapart-c05",
      "source_id": "ascapart-s1",
      "quote": "Alongside the story of Bevis of Hampton, the giant appears in the local folklore of Hampshire and the New Forest; large medieval painted oak panels of Sir Bevis and Ascupart can be found in the Southampton Bargate Monument Gallery",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Folklor Hampshire dan New Forest; panel lukisan Sir Bevis dan Ascupart di Southampton Bargate Monument Gallery.",
        "en": "Folklore of Hampshire and the New Forest; painted panels of Sir Bevis and Ascupart in the Southampton Bargate Monument Gallery."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bánánach adalah makhluk gaib dalam folklor Irlandia awal, hantu yang menghantui medan perang.",
    "en": "The bánánach were preternatural beings of early Irish folklore, spectres that haunted battlefields.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bananach-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Irlandia awal, bánánach adalah makhluk gaib berupa hantu yang menghantui medan perang. Mereka disebut bersama bócanach (\"hantu perang mirip kambing\"), geniti glinne (\"hantu perang perempuan dari lembah\") dan demna aeóir (\"setan udara\") dalam Táin Bó Cúalnge.",
      "en": "In early Irish folklore the bánánach were preternatural beings, spectres haunting the field of battle. The Táin Bó Cúalnge names them together with the bócanach ('goat-like battle spectre'), the geniti glinne ('female battle-spectre of the glen') and the demna aeóir ('demons of the air').",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bananach-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Táin Bó Cúalnge, bánánach, bócanach dan geniti glinne dikirim Tuatha Dé Danann untuk menebar ketakutan pada musuh Cú Chulainn, menjawab teriakan perangnya. Mereka dikaitkan dengan, dan kadang dianggap personifikasi, bunyi pertempuran, dipakai untuk menggambarkan bunyi senjata yang beradu. Ketiganya ditangkal oleh Dond Cúalnge, banteng legendaris dalam Táin.",
      "en": "When Cú Chulainn raises his war cry in the Táin Bó Cúalnge, the Tuatha Dé Danann answer by sending these three spectres to fill his foes with dread. They are tied to, and sometimes seen as personifying, the din of battle, standing for the clash of weapons. All three are kept at bay by the Dond Cúalnge, the legendary bull of the Táin.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bananach-c02",
        "bananach-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Beast of Bont (Bwystfil Bont) adalah binatang legenda, atau khayalan menurut sebagian orang, yang konon menyerang hewan ternak di Ceredigion, Wales.",
    "en": "The Beast of Bont (Bwystfil Bont) is a creature, legendary or imaginary according to some, said to keep attacking farm animals in Ceredigion, Wales.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "beast-of-bont-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bwystfil Bont adalah binatang yang menurut sebagian orang legenda atau khayalan, yang terus menyerang hewan ternak di Ceredigion. Banyak saksi mata mengaku mengenali sosok kucing hitam raksasa di ladang-ladang Ceredigion saat senja. Selama 30 tahun polisi menerima banyak laporan tentang kucing besar mirip puma, panter, macan tutul atau lynx; bukti utamanya adalah tingginya kematian ternak dan bekas cakaran tajam pada kawanan domba.",
      "en": "Bwystfil Bont is a beast, legendary or imaginary by some accounts, that keeps attacking livestock in Ceredigion. Many eyewitnesses say they have made out a huge black cat in Ceredigion's fields as night falls. For 30 years the police have had numerous calls about big cats like a puma, panther, leopard or lynx; the main evidence is the high death rate on farms and the sharp claw marks on sheep.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "beast-of-bont-c01",
        "beast-of-bont-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang Amerika bernama Tom Brown datang ke Wales untuk menyelidiki jejak \"Bwystfil\" itu. Hal yang paling misterius adalah betapa sunyinya serangan itu: dalam satu kejadian, bahu dan kaki seekor domba terpisah dari tubuhnya, sementara kawanan lainnya tetap tidur.",
      "en": "An American named Tom Brown came to Wales to look for traces of the 'Bwystfil'. What is most puzzling is how quietly the attacks happen: in one case a sheep's shoulder and leg were torn from its body while the rest of the flock went on sleeping.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "beast-of-bont-c03",
        "beast-of-bont-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": {

      "en": "Giant black cat.",

    "id": "Dalam satu serangan Beast of Bont, kawanan domba konon tetap tidur meski seekor domba tercabik.",
    "en": "In one Beast of Bont attack the flock reportedly slept on while one sheep was torn apart.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "quote": "Bwystfil - chwedlonol neu ddychmygol yn ôl rhai - yw Bwystfil Bont sy’n parhau i ymosod ar anifeiliaid ffermydd Ceredigion.  Honnir gan nifer o lygad-dystion iddynt adnabod ffigur cath ddu enfawr ar hyd caeau ffermydd Sir Ceredigion wrth iddi nosi.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Bwystfil Bont (Beast of Bont), binatang legenda atau khayalan, menyerang ternak di Ceredigion, Wales; kucing hitam raksasa saat senja.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Biast Bhealach Odail adalah monster atau roh dalam folklor Skotlandia yang menghantui Celah Odal di Pulau Skye.",
    "en": "The Biast Bhealach Odail is a monster or spirit of Scottish folklore haunting Odal Pass on the Isle of Skye.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "biast-bhealach-odail-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Skotlandia, Biast Bhealach Odail (ejaan lama Biasd Bhealach Odail) adalah monster atau roh yang menghantui Celah Odal di Pulau Skye. John Gregorson Campbell, dalam bukunya tahun 1902 tentang sihir dan penglihatan kedua di Skotlandia, menggambarkannya sebagai pengubah wujud nokturnal: kadang lelaki, kadang lelaki berkaki satu, kadang seperti anjing greyhound atau binatang yang berkeliaran, dan kadang terdengar menjerit mengerikan sehingga para pekerja meninggalkan pondok mereka.",
      "en": "Odal Pass on the Isle of Skye is haunted, Scottish folklore says, by a monster or spirit called the Biast Bhealach Odail (older spelling Biasd Bhealach Odail). John Gregorson Campbell, in his 1902 book on witchcraft and second sight in Scotland, describes a night-time shapeshifter seen sometimes as a man, sometimes as a one-legged man, sometimes as a greyhound or prowling beast, and at times heard shrieking so dreadfully that workmen fled their bothies.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "biast-bhealach-odail-c01",
        "biast-bhealach-odail-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para pelancong yang melewati celah itu pada malam hari sering melaporkan diserang. Salah satu korbannya ditemukan tewas di tepi jalan dengan dua luka, di sisi tubuh dan di kaki, dengan tangan menekan setiap luka; menurut Campbell, luka itu dianggap mustahil dibuat manusia. Serangan berhenti setelah jasad itu ditemukan, sehingga Katharine Briggs menduga monster itu hantu orang yang dibunuh dan mencari balas dendam.",
      "en": "Travellers crossing the pass by night often said it attacked them. One victim was found dead by the road with two wounds, in the side and the leg, a hand pressed to each; Campbell says such wounds were thought impossible for a human to inflict. The attacks stopped once the body was found, which led Katharine Briggs to suggest the monster was the ghost of a murdered man seeking revenge.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "biast-bhealach-odail-c03",
        "biast-bhealach-odail-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "id": "Brag adalah goblin pengubah wujud yang nakal dalam folklor Northumbria, sering berwujud kuda atau keledai.",
    "en": "A brag is a mischievous shapeshifting goblin of Northumbrian folklore, often in the form of a horse or donkey.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "brag-folklore-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Brag adalah goblin pengubah wujud yang nakal dalam folklor Northumbria (Northumberland dan Durham), sering berwujud kuda atau keledai. Ia senang membiarkan manusia yang tak curiga menungganginya, lalu melempar mereka ke kolam atau semak dan lari sambil tertawa.",
      "en": "A brag is a mischievous shape-changing goblin of Northumbrian folklore (Northumberland and Durham), often appearing as a horse or donkey. It likes to let unwary people climb on its back, then throws them into a pond or bush and gallops off laughing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brag-folklore-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Contoh terkenal adalah Picktree Brag, yang konon mengambil wujud lain yang ganjil seperti anak sapi berselendang putih di leher, lelaki telanjang tanpa kepala, bahkan empat orang yang memegang selembar kain putih. Brag di Humbleknowe tak pernah terlihat, tetapi mengeluarkan bunyi mengerikan di malam hari.",
      "en": "A well-known example is the Picktree Brag, said to take other strange forms such as a calf with a white handkerchief round its neck, a naked headless man, or even four men holding up a white sheet. The brag at Humbleknowe was never seen but made hideous noises at night.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brag-folklore-c02",
        "brag-folklore-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "traits": [

      "claim_ids": [
        "brown-man-of-the-muirs-c01"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Brown Man of the Muirs adalah kurcaci dalam folklor perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia yang menjadi roh pelindung hewan liar.",
    "en": "The Brown Man of the Muirs is a dwarf of Anglo-Scottish border folklore who acts as guardian spirit of wild animals.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "brown-man-of-the-muirs-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia, Brown Man of the Muirs adalah kurcaci yang menjadi roh pelindung hewan liar. William Henderson, dalam Folklore of the Northern Counties (1879), memuat kisah dari surat sejarawan Robert Surtees kepada Sir Walter Scott: setahun sebelum Pemberontakan Besar, dua pemuda dari Newcastle berburu di padang tinggi di atas Elsdon, dan salah satunya melihat seorang kurcaci yang sangat kuat, berpakaian cokelat seperti pakis layu, berambut merah keriting, berwajah garang, dan bermata menyala seperti mata banteng.",
      "en": "In Anglo-Scottish border folklore the Brown Man of the Muirs is a dwarf who guards the wild creatures. William Henderson, in Folklore of the Northern Counties (1879), gives a tale from the historian Robert Surtees's letter to Sir Walter Scott: a year ahead of the Great Rebellion, a pair of Newcastle lads out shooting on the upland moors above Elsdon stopped to rest, and one saw a very strong, stocky dwarf dressed in brown like withered bracken, with frizzled red hair, a fierce face and eyes glowing like a bull's.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brown-man-of-the-muirs-c01",
        "brown-man-of-the-muirs-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kurcaci itu menegur sang pemburu karena memasuki wilayahnya dan membunuh makhluk-makhluk rakyatnya, mengatakan bahwa ia sendiri hanya makan buah beri, kacang dan apel, lalu mengundangnya pulang. Pemuda itu nyaris menyeberangi sungai ketika suara temannya menghentikannya, dan kurcaci itu menghilang; diyakini bila ia menyeberang ia akan dicabik-cabik. Ia tetap meninggal dalam setahun karena mengabaikan peringatan itu. Walter Scott menduga Brown Man berkerabat dengan duergar Northumberland.",
      "en": "The dwarf rebuked the hunter for trespassing on his lands and killing the creatures who were his subjects, said he himself ate only whortleberries, nuts and apples, and invited him home. The youth was about to leap the brook when his friend's voice stopped him, and the dwarf vanished; had he crossed, it was thought, he would have been torn apart. He died within the year all the same, for ignoring the warning. Walter Scott suggested the Brown Man was akin to the duergar of Northumberland.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brown-man-of-the-muirs-c03",
        "brown-man-of-the-muirs-c04",

      "quote": "In the folklore on the Anglo-Scottish border, the Brown Man of the Muirs is a dwarf who serves as a guardian spirit of wild animals.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brown Man of the Muirs, kurcaci folklor perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia (Anglo-Scottish), roh pelindung hewan liar.",
        "en": "Brown Man of the Muirs, a dwarf of Anglo-Scottish border folklore, guardian spirit of wild animals."

      "source_id": "brown-man-of-the-muirs-s1",
      "quote": "the \"Brown man of the Muirs\", a dwarf very strong and stoutly built, his dress brown like withered bracken, his head covered with frizzled red hair, his countenance ferocious, and his eyes glowing like those of a bull.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "William Henderson, Folklore of the Northern Counties (1879); Robert Surtees, Sir Walter Scott; Pemberontakan Besar (Great Rebellion); Newcastle; Elsdon; kurcaci kuat, berbaju cokelat, rambut merah, mata banteng.",
        "en": "William Henderson, Folklore of the Northern Counties (1879); Robert Surtees, Sir Walter Scott; Great Rebellion; Newcastle; Elsdon; strong dwarf, brown dress, red hair, bull's eyes."

      "source_id": "brown-man-of-the-muirs-s1",

    {
      "id": "brown-man-of-the-muirs-c04",
      "source_id": "brown-man-of-the-muirs-s1",
      "quote": "It was thought that had the young man crossed the water the dwarf would have torn him to pieces. As it was he died within the year",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Would have been torn apart if he crossed; died within the year."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bucca adalah roh laut lelaki dalam folklor Cornwall, sejenis manusia ikan yang menghuni tambang dan permukiman pesisir sebagai hobgoblin saat badai.",
    "en": "The Bucca is a male sea-spirit of Cornish folklore, a merman haunting mines and coastal communities as a hobgoblin in storms.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bucca-mythological-creature-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bucca (Cornish: bocka, jamak bockas) adalah roh laut lelaki dalam folklor Cornwall, manusia ikan yang menghuni tambang dan permukiman pesisir sebagai hobgoblin saat badai. Ia sejenis roh air yang kemungkinan berkerabat dengan Púca Irlandia, Pwca Welsh, dan mari-morgan perempuan dari mitologi Welsh dan Breton. Pendeta W. S. Lach-Szyrma menduga Bucca dulunya dewa laut pagan seperti Nechtan Irlandia atau Nodens Britania, meski itu sebagian besar dugaan.",
      "en": "The Bucca (Cornish bocka, plural bockas) is a male sea-spirit of Cornish folklore, a merman who haunted mines and coastal communities as a hobgoblin during storms. It is a water spirit probably akin to the Irish Púca, the Welsh Pwca and the female mari-morgans of Welsh and Breton myth. The Rev. W. S. Lach-Szyrma proposed that the Bucca was once a pagan sea god like the Irish Nechtan or British Nodens, though this is largely conjecture.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bucca-mythological-creature-c01",
        "bucca-mythological-creature-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bucca disebut dalam buku bahasa Cornwall Gwreans an bys (1611). Folkloris William Bottrell (1890) mencatat orang tua menyebut Bucka Gwidden (roh baik) dan Bucka Dhu (roh jahat), dan nelayan Newlyn dan Mousehole biasa meninggalkan sebagian tangkapan di pasir pada malam hari untuk Bucka. Di Penzance, badai dari barat daya disebut \"Bucca calling\", dan pelaut percaya suara Bucca terbawa angin.",
      "en": "The Bucca is mentioned in the Cornish book Gwreans an bys (1611). The folklorist William Bottrell (1890) noted that old people spoke of a Bucka Gwidden, a good spirit, and a Bucka Dhu, an evil one, and that Newlyn and Mousehole fishermen used to leave part of their catch on the sand at night for Bucka. In Penzance south-westerly storms were called 'Bucca calling', and seamen believed his voice rode the wind.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bucca-mythological-creature-c03",
        "bucca-mythological-creature-c04",
        "bucca-mythological-creature-c05"
      ]
    }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Caoránach adalah Oilliphéist dalam folklor Irlandia, \"ibu para setan\" yang dibuang Santo Patrick ke Lough Dearg di Donegal.",
    "en": "Caoránach is an Oilliphéist of Irish folklore, the 'mother of demons' banished by Saint Patrick to Lough Dearg in Donegal.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "caoranach-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam folklor Irlandia, Caoránach (kadang Caol) adalah seekor Oilliphéist, ular monster, yang disebut ibu para setan dan dibuang Santo Patrick ke Lough Dearg di Donegal, Ulster. Menurut legenda yang lebih tua, Fionn mac Cumhaill dan Fianna diminta membunuh seorang nenek sihir di daerah Lough Dearg; mereka diperingatkan agar tidak mematahkan tulang pahanya, tetapi seorang bernama Conan tetap mematahkannya dan melepaskan cacing kecil berbulu bernama Caoránach.",
      "en": "In Irish folklore Caoránach (sometimes Caol) was an Oilliphéist, a monstrous serpent, called the mother of demons and banished by Saint Patrick to Lough Dearg in Donegal, Ulster. In older legend Fionn mac Cumhaill and the Fianna were asked to kill a hag near Lough Dearg; warned not to break her thigh bone, a man named Conan broke it anyway and freed a small hairy worm named Caoránach.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "caoranach-c01",
        "caoranach-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cacing itu cepat tumbuh menjadi monster besar yang berusaha memakan semua ternak di negeri itu; Conan, yang disalahkan rakyat Ulster, menikamnya dari dalam mulutnya. Lough Dearg dinamai menurut batu-batunya yang merah, konon dicelup darah Caoránach. Dalam versi yang lebih Kristen, Santo Patrick membunuh monster itu dan darahnya memerahkan danau. Chris Cairney menduga kisah ini turut mengilhami legenda Monster Loch Ness.",
      "en": "The worm soon grew into a huge monster trying to devour all the cattle in the land; Conan, blamed by the people of Ulster, stabbed it from inside its mouth. Lough Dearg takes its name from its red rocks, said to be stained by Caoránach's blood. In a more Christian telling Saint Patrick slays the monster and its blood reddens the lake. Chris Cairney believes the tale helped inspire the Loch Ness Monster legend.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "caoranach-c01",
      "source_id": "caoranach-s1",
      "quote": "In Irish folklore, Caoránach (sometimes Caol) was an Oilliphéist and said to be the mother of demons who was banished by Saint Patrick to Lough Dearg in Donegal, Ulster.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Caoránach (Caol), Oilliphéist folklor Irlandia (Irish), ibu para setan, dibuang Santo Patrick (Saint Patrick) ke Lough Dearg, Donegal, Ulster.",
        "en": "Caoránach (Caol), an Oilliphéist of Irish folklore, mother of demons, banished by Saint Patrick to Lough Dearg, Donegal, Ulster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "caoranach-c02",
      "source_id": "caoranach-s1",
      "quote": "A man named Conan breaks the bone nevertheless, and releases a small hairy worm. The worm, named Caoránach, quickly grows into a large monster which attempts to eat all the cattle in the land.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fionn mac Cumhaill dan Fianna; Conan mematahkan tulang paha nenek sihir; cacing berbulu Caoránach memakan ternak.",
        "en": "Fionn mac Cumhaill and the Fianna; Conan breaks the hag's thigh bone; hairy worm Caoránach eats cattle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "caoranach-c03",
      "source_id": "caoranach-s1",
      "quote": "The people of Ulster blame Conan for the deaths of their cattle. Enraged, Conan stabs the monster from within its mouth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Conan menikam monster dari dalam mulutnya.",
        "en": "Conan stabs the monster from inside its mouth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "caoranach-c04",
      "source_id": "caoranach-s1",
      "quote": "Lough Dearg is named after its red rocks, which are said to have been dyed by Caoránach's blood. In a more Christianised version of the story, Saint Patrick slays the monster after being told about it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lough Dearg dinamai menurut batu merah yang dicelup darah Caoránach; versi Kristen: Santo Patrick membunuhnya.",
        "en": "Lough Dearg named for red rocks dyed by Caoránach's blood; Christian version: Saint Patrick slays it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "caoranach-c05",
      "source_id": "caoranach-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed by Chris Cairney that this story and one involving Caoránach helped influence and inspire the legend of the Loch Ness Monster.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chris Cairney: mengilhami legenda Monster Loch Ness (Loch Ness Monster).",
        "en": "Chris Cairney: helped inspire the Loch Ness Monster legend."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Naga Christchurch adalah legenda kota Christchurch, Dorset, tentang naga berkepala lima yang menghancurkan gereja dan sebagian besar kota.",
    "en": "The Christchurch Dragon is a legend of Christchurch, Dorset, about a five-headed dragon that destroyed the church and much of the town.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "christchurch-dragon-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga Christchurch adalah legenda yang dikaitkan dengan kota Christchurch, Dorset, di pesisir selatan Inggris. Legenda ini berasal dari karya Latin pertengahan abad ke-12 oleh Hermann de Tournai, yang menceritakan bagaimana rombongan kanon dari Katedral Notre-Dame di Laon, Prancis, menyaksikan naga berkepala lima menghancurkan gereja dan sebagian besar kota. Meski terdokumentasi baik, legenda ini kurang dikenal di kota asalnya.",
      "en": "The Christchurch Dragon is a legend tied to the town of Christchurch, Dorset, on England's south coast. It comes from a mid-12th-century Latin work by Hermann de Tournai, in which visiting canons of Laon's Notre-Dame cathedral in France watch a dragon with five heads lay waste to the church and most of the town. Though well documented, it is little known in the town itself.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "christchurch-dragon-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sembilan kanon yang sedang menggalang dana ditolak berteduh oleh dekan gereja, yang khawatir relikui Perawan Maria mereka akan menarik lebih banyak persembahan. Setelah mereka pergi, naga yang terbang dari laut membakar rumah-rumah satu per satu dengan nyala api belerang dari kelima kepalanya, tetapi penginapan para pedagang yang menampung mereka tetap utuh, sedangkan gereja hancur total dan perahu berisi harta sang dekan menjadi abu. Latar belakang perjalanan para kanon dicatat Guibert de Nogent dalam Monodies.",
      "en": "Nine canons on a fund-raising tour were refused shelter by the minster's dean, who feared their relics of the Virgin Mary would draw more offerings than his altars. After they left, a dragon flying in from the sea set houses alight one by one with sulphurous flames from its five heads, yet the lodging house of the merchants who had sheltered them was untouched, while the church was wholly destroyed and the dean's treasure-laden boat burned to ashes. Guibert de Nogent's Monodies records the background to the canons' journey.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cirein-cròin adalah monster laut raksasa dalam folklor Gaelik Skotlandia, begitu besar hingga konon memakan tujuh paus.",
    "en": "The Cirein-cròin is a huge sea monster of Scottish Gaelic folklore, so large it was said to feed on seven whales.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cirein-croin-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ceirean, Cirein-cròin atau cionarain-crò adalah monster laut besar dalam folklor Gaelik Skotlandia; sebuah pepatah lama menyatakan ia begitu besar hingga memakan tujuh paus. Menurut folklor setempat, ia dapat menyamar sebagai ikan perak kecil ketika bertemu nelayan; ada kisah yang menyebut penyamaran itu untuk memancing mangsa, sebab begitu ditangkap dan dinaikkan ke perahu, ia kembali ke wujud monster dan memakan nelayan itu.",
      "en": "Ceirean, Cirein-cròin or cionarain-crò was a great sea monster of Scottish Gaelic folklore; an old saying claims it was big enough to feed on seven whales. Local lore says it could take the form of a little silver fish when fishermen met it; some accounts say this was a lure, for once caught and hauled aboard it turned back into the monster and devoured the fisherman.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cirein-croin-c01",
        "cirein-croin-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pepatah yang dicatat Alexander Robert Forbes berbunyi: tujuh herring mengenyangkan salem, tujuh salem mengenyangkan anjing laut, tujuh anjing laut mengenyangkan paus besar, dan tujuh paus mengenyangkan cirein-cròin. Dalam Carmina Gadelica, Alexander Carmichael mencatat versi lain dari Kenneth Morrison di Pulau Skye pada 1860, dan secara tentatif menyamakan cionarain-crò dengan kraken.",
      "en": "A saying recorded by Alexander Robert Forbes runs: seven herrings fill a salmon, seven salmon a seal, seven seals a great whale, and seven whales a cirein-cròin. In Carmina Gadelica Alexander Carmichael recorded another version from Kenneth Morrison on the Isle of Skye in 1860, and tentatively equated the cionarain-crò with the kraken.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cirein-croin-c03",
        "cirein-croin-c04",
        "cirein-croin-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirein-cr%C3%B2in",
      "title": "Cirein-cròin",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "cirein-croin-c01",
      "source_id": "cirein-croin-s1",
      "quote": "Ceirean, Cirein-cròin or cionarain-crò was a large sea monster in Scottish Gaelic folklore. An old saying claims that it was so large that it fed on seven whales",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ceirean, Cirein-cròin, cionarain-crò, monster laut besar folklor Gaelik Skotlandia (Scottish Gaelic); memakan tujuh paus.",
        "en": "Ceirean, Cirein-cròin, cionarain-crò, a large sea monster of Scottish Gaelic folklore; fed on seven whales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cirein-croin-c02",
      "source_id": "cirein-croin-s1",
      "quote": "Local folklores say this huge animal can disguise itself as a small silver fish when fishermen came in contact with it. Other accounts state the reason for the disguise was to attract its next meal",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menyamar sebagai ikan perak kecil untuk memancing mangsa; memakan nelayan.",
        "en": "Disguised as a small silver fish to lure prey; ate the fisherman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cirein-croin-c03",
      "source_id": "cirein-croin-s1",
      "quote": "Seachd mial, sath Cirein-cròin Seven whales, a cirein-cròin's fill",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pepatah Alexander Robert Forbes: tujuh paus mengenyangkan cirein-cròin; herring, salem, anjing laut.",
        "en": "Alexander Robert Forbes's saying: seven whales fill a cirein-cròin; herrings, salmon, seals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cirein-croin-c04",
      "source_id": "cirein-croin-s1",
      "quote": "In Carmina Gadelica, Alexander Carmichael attested this version to a cottar named Kenneth Morrison, in Trithion, on the Isle of Skye, from whom it was recorded in 1860.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Carmina Gadelica, Alexander Carmichael; Kenneth Morrison, Pulau Skye (Isle of Skye), 1860.",
        "en": "Carmina Gadelica, Alexander Carmichael; Kenneth Morrison, Isle of Skye, 1860."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cirein-croin-c05",
      "source_id": "cirein-croin-s1",
      "quote": "(Carmichael tentatively identified the \"cionarain-cro\" with the \"kracken\".)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Carmichael menyamakan cionarain-crò dengan kraken (kracken).",
        "en": "Carmichael tentatively identified the cionarain-crò with the kraken."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Clṻd adalah dewi Brittonik Sungai Clyde yang keberadaannya dihipotesiskan atau disimpulkan dalam mitologi Kelt.",
    "en": "Clṻd is a hypothesised, inferred Brittonic goddess of the River Clyde in Celtic mythology.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "clud-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Kelt, Clṻd atau Clọ:tā (Latin: Clōta) adalah dewi Brittonik Sungai Clyde yang dihipotesiskan. Bila pernah dipuja, ia mungkin dipuja suku Damnonii yang menguasai lembah Clyde, kelak Kerajaan Alt Clud dan Strathclyde. Nama \"Clṻd\" adalah bentuk Brittonik rekonstruksi untuk nama sungai Clyde; James (2019) mengartikan Clṻd \"murni, bersih\" dan Clọ:tā \"ia yang murni\" sebagai kemungkinan nama sang dewi.",
      "en": "In Celtic mythology Clṻd or Clọ:tā (Latin Clōta) is a hypothesised Brittonic goddess of the River Clyde. If she was ever worshipped, it was presumably by the Damnonii, who held the Clyde basin that later became the Kingdom of Alt Clud and then Strathclyde. 'Clṻd' is the reconstructed Brittonic form of the river name Clyde; James (2019) glosses Clṻd as 'pure, cleansed' and offers Clọ:tā, 'she who is pure', as a possible name for the goddess.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "clud-c01",
        "clud-c02",
        "clud-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hampir tidak ada bukti langsung tentang keberadaan atau pemujaan dewi ini. Pada 1967 sebuah patung Kelt berkepala tiga ditemukan dekat Clyde, yang menurut Ross (1974) sangat mungkin menggambarkan dewa, menunjukkan pemujaan dewa air di daerah itu. Sir John Rhŷs (1891) menunjuk tokoh Gwawl ap Clud dalam Mabinogi, tunangan pertama Rhiannon, dan mengusulkan ia putra dewi Clud.",
      "en": "There is little if any direct evidence that this deity existed or was worshipped. In 1967 a three-headed Celtic statue was found near the Clyde, which Ross (1974) reads as very likely depicting a god, pointing to the worship of water deities there. Sir John Rhŷs (1891) pointed to Gwawl ap Clud, Rhiannon's first betrothed in the Mabinogi, proposing he was the son of the goddess Clud.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "clud-c04",
        "clud-c05",
        "clud-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
